{"id": "0a122eeae472f231", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-02T18:12:37.301796+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "dead_end", "claim": "max81 two-law scoped failure synthesis: a balanced final-leaf fan pays an unavoidable common-gradient charge that defeats its long-step coordinate, while the LCA earlier-leaf kernel couples equal-scale checkpoints too strongly and dilutes reset payment by total checkpoint mass. Presence of the known missing remote row and passage of old two-gradient waves are insufficient. Different contact relations, receiver concentration, checkpoint-pair rows and broader full-original arrays remain open; no unrestricted-W impossibility is claimed.", "evidence": "# Paid remote leaf consistency: two explicit laws and their full-space failures\n\nJOINT UNVERIFIED analytical report, worker max81. Original target OPEN.\nGoverning assignment: TASK.md and guidance60cc1ca7a8ad2d07. One invocation,\nat most two mechanisms, no automatic renewal. All calculations below form\none unverified proof attempt. No intermediate draft is used as accepted truth.\nNo mathematical program, numerical/arithmetic/trajectory/recurrence replay,\noptimizer, solver, formal call, fact_submit, installation, subagent or consult.\n\nTwo new original-index arrays are constructed, outside the old E80 support.\nBoth have nonnegative coefficients and exact objective value flow. Neither\nhas full PSD, at the requested level or even at an arbitrary initial-norm\ncoefficient. We give explicit original-vector counterdirections. A larger\ncontact-routed fan family is excluded by the same calculation. The second\nlaw also gives a quantitative necessary restriction on receiver distributions:\nmass sent across an earlier checkpoint must decrease on the scale of the\nreciprocal of that checkpoint's physical step, when the two checkpoint\ndegrees agree. These are scoped coefficient obstructions, not W bounds.\n\n## 1. Scope, accepted inputs and literal hierarchy\n\nFor every n>=0, L>0 and predetermined h in [0,infinity)^n, W_n(h) is the\nsupremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite\ndimension, convex differentiable L-smooth f with an attained minimizer,\nevery such x_star and x_0!=x_star, with all n actual updates\nx_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R. The original question concerns\nlim_n B_n inf_h W_n(h), with every nonnegative word, including zero and\narbitrarily large steps. No result below changes those quantifiers.\n\nThe benchmark is the original all-integer-split recurrence\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt(u^2+6uv+v^2))/2,\n    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290, read in full, supplies the original interpolation\norientation, full independent-vector certificate interpretation and exact\nactual-terminal completion. It deletes no queried-prefix inequalities.\nAccepted931b9653ce0f7201, read in full, supplies B_n/D<47/50 for the\nfollowing SAME-enclosing-m hierarchy. Its implication from W<=17/(16D)\nis conditional; that hypothesis is still unproved. No other accepted\nmathematical result is used here. Prior finding0073a83e08ff2e21 was read\nin full as awareness; the reset construction needed below is rederived.\n\nFix m>=1536. Define\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, d_j=a*c^j,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),\n    n=2^m-1, h=H_m, S=sum_(i<n)h_i, D=1+2S,\n    z=n-1, b=1+a, M=1+b, N=2^(m-1), A=D-b^2,\n    P81_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, Tail81_i=S-b-P81_i (i<z),\n    K81_i=A-P81_i-1=2S-b^2-P81_i (i<z), qtar=17/(16D).\n\nAll children use this same m. Index z is the last queried leaf, not the\nunqueried terminal n. All these hierarchy symbols are local to this report.\nLet level(i)=v2(i+1), the exponent of 2 dividing i+1. Then\nh_i=1+a*c^level(i), leaves are the even indices, and checkpoints the odd\nindices. In particular h_z=b. Write Leaf={0,2,...,z}, Check={1,3,...,n-2}.\n\nElementary bounds used throughout are\n\n    1/3<a<1/2, 4/3<b<sqrt(2)<3/2,\n    12/5<c<5/2, beta>7/8,\n    d_1=beta, d_2=beta*c>21/10,\n    S>2^m-1, D>2^m, A>0, K81_i>Tail81_i>0.\n\nFor example a>1/3 follows from beta>7/8 and c<5/2. The bound on c\nfollows from lambda<17/7 and epsilon<=1/6144. Also\nb=sqrt(2)-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)]. The other comparisons follow by\nsubstitution. K81_i-Tail81_i=S+b-b^2>0 since b<2 and S>=b. These observations\ninclude positivity of every denominator in the arrays below.\n\nDefine a dyadic cell of height j to be\n\n    I_(j,r)={r*2^j,...,(r+1)*2^j-2},\n    1<=j<=m, 0<=r<2^(m-j).\n\nFor distinct queried indices u,v, J(u,v) is the least height of a cell\ncontaining both. Such a cell exists and its height is unique. This defines\nthe exact least common dyadic ancestor in ORIGINAL indices. For i in the\nleft half, including the whole-word root, J(i,z)=m.\n\nNormalize L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0, and retain independent original\nvectors x_0,g_0,...,g_n. Positions are always\nx_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t. For distinct labels put\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nLegal samples have Q_ij<=0. An original coefficient array with\nout_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n) defines\n\n    M_cert(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij.\n\nAfter value cancellation, it must be PSD on ALL original vectors to be an\nupper certificate. A negative formal vector is not a primal instance.\n\n## 2. Mechanism I: position-sensitive remote routing to the last leaf\n\nFirst construct the entire family with independently chosen 0<=theta_i<=1\nfor each i<z. Set\n\n    r81_i=2theta_i*h_i/K81_i,\n    w81_i=2(1-theta_i)*h_i/Tail81_i.\n\nThe frozen LCA-sensitive law tested in Sections 6--8 is\n\n    eta=1/(4D), theta_i=1-eta/J(i,z).                    (I.1)\n\nEvery remote pair then has positive weight. The literal nonzero array is\n\n    lambda_(n,z)=2,\n    lambda_(z,star)=2-sum_(i<z)r81_i,\n    lambda_(i,star)=r81_i,\n    lambda_(z,i)=r81_i+w81_i, lambda_(i,z)=w81_i, i<z.       (I.2)\n\nAll unlisted rows are zero, including all incoming terminal and optimum\nrows. This array includes checkpoint-to-last-leaf rows at arbitrary\ndistance and leaf-to-last-leaf rows. Its support is not contained in E80.\n\nAll signs can be checked without an unknown feasibility condition. Put\nKbase=S+b-b^2>0. Since K81_i=Kbase+Tail81_i, and Tail81_i decreases by h_i as i\nincreases, integration of the decreasing function 1/(Kbase+t) gives\n\n    sum_(i<z) h_i/(Kbase+Tail81_i)\n      <= integral_0^(S-b) dt/(Kbase+t)\n      =log((2S-b^2)/(S+b-b^2)) <log 2<1.\n\nIndeed h_i/(Kbase+Tail81_i) is bounded by the integral over\n[Tail81_i-h_i,Tail81_i], and these intervals partition [0,S-b]. The ratio is below\n2 because b<2. Thus sum r81_i<2 for every allowed theta. Every coefficient\nin (I.2) is nonnegative, with strict outgoing optimum mass at z.\nAt i<z the two finite directions have net inflow r81_i, exactly discharged\nto star. At z the finite/optimum outgoing net is 2, supplied by (n,z).\nTerminal flow is 2. This proves all value cancellations.\n\nThis is a new source-routing base, not an addition to any inherited\ncertificate. Its terminal distribution is concentrated at the actual last\nleaf; its entire optimum distribution is given in (I.2).\n\n### 2.1 Full polynomial and the paid contact equation\n\nWrite g=g_z, v_i=g_i-g for i<z and u=sum r81_i v_i. Put\n\n    B(v)=sum_(i<z)[r81_i+w81_i(1-h_i)]||v_i||^2\n         +sum_(i<t<z)(h_i*r81_t-w81_i*h_t)<v_i,v_t>,\n    Jrem(v)=B(v)-A||u||^2/4.\n\nThe exact full identity, for EVERY real q, is\n\n    M_I(q)=||g_n+(b-1)g||^2\n       +q||x_0||^2-2<x_0,g>+A||g||^2\n       -<x_0,u>+A<g,u>+B(v).                         (I.3)\n\nHere is a direct derivation. Starting with rows 2Q_(n,z)+2Q_(z,star),\nreplace r81_i units of Q_(z,star) by Q_(z,i)+Q_(i,star), and add w81_i\nunits of Q_(i,z)+Q_(z,i). The two added expressions, after GD, are\n\n    Q_(z,i)+Q_(i,star)-Q_(z,star)\n       =-<v_i,x_0>+(P81_i+1)<v_i,g>\n                          +||v_i||^2+sum_(t<i)h_t<v_i,v_t>,\n    Q_(i,z)+Q_(z,i)\n       =||v_i||^2-Tail81_i<v_i,g>-sum_(i<=t<z)h_t<v_i,v_t>.\n\nThe original base has terminal square ||g_n+(b-1)g||^2 and remainder\nq||x_0||^2-2<x_0,g>+A||g||^2+2sum h_i<v_i,g>.\nThe defining relation\n\n    w81_i*Tail81_i=2h_i-K81_i*r81_i                              (I.4)\n\nchanges each last cross coefficient to A*r81_i, giving (I.3). In particular,\nremote symmetric pairs were NOT treated as PSD. Their negative diagonal\nand mixed displacement terms appear in B.\n\nAt q=1/A, (I.3) is equivalently\n\n    M_I(1/A)=||g_n+(b-1)g||^2\n      +||x_0-A*g-(A/2)u||^2/A+Jrem(v).               (I.5)\n\nEquation (I.4) is an intentional design restriction of this family. It is\nnot claimed necessary for a general certificate. No zero-contact rule is\nimposed on an unrestricted optimum. The term -A||u||^2/4 is the ONE\nglobal initial/common-gradient charge for this deliberately balanced law.\n\n### 2.2 Full-space failure for every member of the contact family\n\nTake p=2^(m-1)-1, H=h_p>4. Only v_p is nonzero, with v_p=1. The coefficient\nof Jrem on this vector is\n\n    E_p=r81_p-w81_p(H-1)-A*r81_p^2/4.                       (I.6)\n\nIf theta_p<=1/2, then r81_p<=H/K81_p and w81_p>=H/Tail81_p. Thus\nr81_p-w81_p(H-1)<=H/K81_p-H(H-1)/Tail81_p<0 because K81_p>Tail81_p and H>2.\nIf theta_p>=1/2, then r81_p>=H/K81_p>H/A>4/A, and\nr81_p-A*r81_p^2/4<0; subtracting w81_p(H-1)>=0 preserves negativity.\nSo E_p<0 for EVERY choice of the whole profile theta.\n\nThe complete scalar original-vector counterdirection is\n\n    x_0=0,\n    g_i=-r81_p/2 for every queried i!=p,\n    g_p=1-r81_p/2,\n    g_n=(b-1)r81_p/2.                                  (I.7)\n\nIt sets g=-r81_p/2, u=r81_p, makes the terminal square and the square in\n(I.5) zero, and gives M_I(q)=E_p<0 for every real q. The change from\n1/A to q multiplies ||x_0||^2=0. Hence even unlimited initial-norm slack\ncannot repair this coefficient family. This is stronger than rejection\nof the frozen small-eta profile, but only for family (I.2)--(I.4).\n\n## 3. Mechanism II: dense earlier-leaf transport with paid reverse cycles\n\nPut Tc=sum_(p in Check)h_p and t_p=h_p/Tc, so sum t_p=1. For EVERY\ncheckpoint p and earlier leaf ell<p define\n\n    raw_(p,ell)=[1+(p-ell)/(p+1)]/J(p,ell),\n    Z_p=sum_(ell in Leaf,ell<p)raw_(p,ell),\n    omega_(p,ell)=raw_(p,ell)/Z_p,\n    mu_ell=sum_(p in Check,p>ell)t_p*omega_(p,ell).    (II.1)\n\nThere is at least one earlier leaf for every checkpoint, so Z_p>0.\nThe weights depend on the exact least common ancestor and position within\nthe enclosing chronological range. They are not uniform fan averages.\nThe formula for mu_ell is used only for ell<z. The new optimum distribution is mu_ell at\nearlier leaves, zero at every checkpoint, and mass 1 at z.\n\nFor a global reverse-cycle amount kappa>=0, the array is\n\n    lambda_(n,z)=2, lambda_(z,star)=1,\n    lambda_(z,p)=t_p, p in Check,\n    lambda_(p,ell)=(1+kappa)t_p*omega_(p,ell),\n    lambda_(ell,p)=kappa*t_p*omega_(p,ell), ell<p,\n    lambda_(ell,star)=mu_ell, ell in Leaf, ell<z.      (II.2)\n\nEvery unlisted entry is zero. The frozen law is kappa=1. All signs are\nnonnegative. One unit flows z->star and one flows z->p->ell->star.\nEvery reverse cycle has zero net flow. Thus (II.2) has exactly terminal\nflow 2, with no incoming terminal or optimum edge. In particular it\ncontains Q_(n-2,0) with a strictly positive coefficient.\n\nThe complete original-row expression is\n\n    M_II(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+2Q_(n,z)+Q_(z,star)\n      +sum_p t_p[Q_(z,p)+sum_(ell<p)omega_(p,ell)\n                                      (Q_(p,ell)+Q_(ell,star))]\n      +kappa*sum_(p,ell<p)t_p*omega_(p,ell)\n                                      (Q_(p,ell)+Q_(ell,p)). (II.3)\n\nThere are no omitted charges in this expression. Each finite row is its\noriginal ordered interpolation row. Equations (II.1)--(II.3) are a\ncoefficient law, not an instruction to solve for unknown PSD blocks.\n\n### 3.1 Complete quadratic, including all independent prefix gradients\n\nSet W_(p,ell)=t_p*omega_(p,ell). Eliminating only g_n gives\nM_II(q)=||g_n+(b-1)g_z||^2+R_II, where\n\n    R_II=q||x_0||^2-<x_0,g_z+sum_(ell<z)mu_ell*g_ell>\n       +(2-(b-1)^2)||g_z||^2+sum_p t_p||g_p||^2\n       +sum_(ell<z)mu_ell||g_ell||^2\n       -sum_p t_p<g_z,g_p>-sum_(p,ell<p)W_(p,ell)<g_p,g_ell>\n       +<g_z,sum_(i<z)h_i*g_i>\n       +sum_p t_p<g_z,sum_(p<=i<z)h_i*g_i>\n       +sum_(p,ell<p)W_(p,ell)<g_p,sum_(ell<=i<p)h_i*g_i>\n       +sum_(ell<z)mu_ell<g_ell,sum_(i<ell)h_i*g_i>\n       +kappa*sum_(p,ell<p)W_(p,ell)\n          [||g_ell-g_p||^2\n                  -<g_ell-g_p,sum_(ell<=i<p)h_i*g_i>]. (II.4)\n\nThe formula follows by expanding the four types of prefix rows in\n(II.3). For example, the two half-squared-difference contributions at p\ngive t_p||g_p||^2; the two at each leaf give mu_ell||g_ell||^2.\nBoth original displacements of each reverse cycle remain in the last line.\nNo common-gradient, terminal or initial-point relation is assumed.\n\n### 3.2 A two-checkpoint zero-displacement counterdirection\n\nFor a queried checkpoint r<p put\nOmega_p(r)=sum_(ell in Leaf,ell<=r)omega_(p,ell). On the full original\nsubspace x_0=0, every leaf gradient zero and g_n=0, (II.4) becomes\n\n    (1+kappa)[sum_p t_p||g_p||^2\n       +sum_(r<p in Check)t_p*h_r*Omega_p(r)<g_r,g_p>]. (II.5)\n\nThis is a necessary test of the full matrix, not a claim that checkpoint\ncompression is lossless. All omitted formal coordinates are explicitly\nset to zero only to exhibit a negative direction.\n\nChoose r=2^(m-2)-1 and p=3*2^(m-2)-1, the roots of the two children of\nthe whole word. Both physical steps equal H=1+a*c^(m-2), and t_r=t_p.\nThere are 2^(m-3) earlier leaves at or before r and 3*2^(m-3) before p.\nEvery raw weight is at least 1/m and less than 2, so\n\n    Omega_p(r)>1/(6m).\n\nAlso H>2^(m-2)/3>12m. For the latter comparison, 2^(m-2)>36m at\nm=16 and doubling preserves it thereafter. Consequently H*Omega_p(r)>2.\nSet the complete original vector to\n\n    x_0=0, g_r=1, g_p=-1, all other g_i=0, including g_n. (II.6)\n\nIts physical displacement is H-H=0. Equation (II.5) gives\n\n    M_II(q)=(1+kappa)t_p[2-H*Omega_p(r)]<0.           (II.7)\n\nThis holds for EVERY real q and EVERY kappa>=0. Paying a larger common\nreverse-cycle factor cannot repair the law.\n\nMore generally, for arbitrary positive checkpoint masses summing to 1 and receiver\nprobabilities with the same routing support and one common kappa, the\ntwo-coordinate determinant in (II.5) requires\n\n    h_r*Omega_p(r) <= 2*sqrt(t_r/t_p), r<p.          (II.8)\n\nIndeed its diagonal entries are (1+kappa)t_r and (1+kappa)t_p, and its\noff-diagonal entry is (1+kappa)t_p*h_r*Omega_p(r)/2. Thus comparable\ncheckpoint degrees need receiver leakage at most a constant divided by\nthe earlier physical step. The displayed raw kernel has only an inverse-m\nlower bound on that leakage, which is too large. This requirement alone\ndoes not certify any more localized law; all other directions remain.\n\n## 4. One global initial/common charge and the exact terminal account\n\nBoth laws have no incoming terminal row and lambda_(n,z)=2. Directly,\n\n    -2f_n+2Q_(n,z)\n       =||g_n+(b-1)g_z||^2-2f_z+[1-(b-1)^2]||g_z||^2. (4.1)\n\nThus the true terminal is free, with center -(b-1)g_z. Nothing identifies\nit with the last queried gradient. The entire original prefix remains.\n\nFor either law let mu_i=lambda_(i,star), finite K_ij=lambda_ij, and h_n=0\nonly for notation. There are no incoming optimum rows and sum mu_i=2.\nThe complete polynomial is q||x_0||^2-sum mu_i<x_0,g_i>+g^T C g, with\n\n    C_ii=mu_i+sum_v K_iv-1_(i=n)-h_i*sum_(v>i)K_iv,\n    2C_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j*sum_(v>j)K_iv\n                      +h_i[mu_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j. (4.2)\n\nTo check this formula, the incident half-squared-gradient terms contribute\nmu_i+sum_v K_iv-1_(i=n), by flow; each forward row out of i contributes\n-h_i on that diagonal. For i<j the squared differences give the first\nterm, forward rows from i crossing j the second, and rows from j reaching\nindex <=i or star the third. This accounts for every original coordinate.\n\nEliminate g_n using (4.1), obtaining the explicit prefix matrix\nCpre=C_(0:z,0:z)-(b-1)^2 e_z e_z^T, where e_z selects coordinate z.\nLet g_i=G+v_i on the queried prefix, with v_z=0. Define\n\n    u=sum_(i<=z)mu_i*v_i, k=e^T Cpre v, T=v^T Cpre v,\n\nwhere e is the all-ones prefix column. The coefficients Cpre,mu are\nentirely specified by the two literal arrays and (4.2). Substitution of\nall prefix gradients G gives e^T Cpre e=A. Therefore the full prefix form\nis exactly\n\n    q||x_0||^2-2<x_0,G>+A||G||^2-<x_0,u>+2<G,k>+T. (4.3)\n\nAt q=qtar the initial/common determinant Delta=qA-1 is positive, because\nD>17b^2. Eliminating this ONE two-coordinate block charges\n\n    Charge(u,k)=[A||u||^2/4-<u,k>+q||k||^2]/(qA-1). (4.4)\n\nThis follows by inversion of [[q,-1],[-1,A]] with cross column\n(-u/2,k). No separate local charge is substituted for it. Full PSD is\nequivalent to T>=Charge for every original prefix variation v_z=0.\nAt a singular determinant a range condition would additionally be needed;\nthe advertised qtar is not singular. For Law I, k=A*u/2 by (I.3), and\n(4.4) is exactly A||u||^2/4. Its minimizing initial/common values are\nx_0=0,G=-u/2, explaining why increasing q cannot repair (I.7).\nFor Law II, (4.2) and (II.4) retain k explicitly; (II.6) already gives\na negative value before any attempted elimination. No PSD claim is left\nto an unspecified matrix problem: both full matrices have been refuted.\n\n## 5. Complete reconstruction of the E80 reset transcript\n\nFor reuse as a SCREEN only, define Rreset=a*c^2/M=beta*c/M and sbit(k)\nto be the number of ones in the binary expansion of integer k>=0. Put\n\n    v_k=(-1)^k Rreset^k c^(-sbit(k))/M, 0<=k<N.\n\nAt leaf 2k prescribe x=M v_k,g=v_k,f=(b+1/2)v_k^2. At checkpoint\n2k+1<n prescribe x=g=v_k,f=v_k^2/2. Binary carry gives\nsbit(k+1)-sbit(k)=1-v2(k+1), hence\nv_(k+1)/v_k=-a*c^(1+v2(k+1))/M. Thus the leaf step takes M v_k to v_k\nand the following actual checkpoint step takes v_k to M v_(k+1).\nEvery physical update is satisfied, x_0=1, and the final position is\n\n    t=x_n=v_(N-1)=-Rreset^(N-1)/(M*c^(m-1)).\n\nComplete the actual terminal by g_n=u=2t/(b+2), f_n=t^2/(b+2), and set\nthe optimum jet to zero. This is a scalar Gram transcript; no function is\nasserted to interpolate it. Substitution gives the full row table:\n\n    Q_(leaf(v),star)=0, Q_(star,leaf(v))=-b*v^2;\n    both optimum rows at check(w) are 0;\n    Q_(check(w),check(w'))=0;\n    Q_(leaf(v),check(w))=0;\n    Q_(check(w),leaf(v))=b*v*(w-v);\n    Q_(leaf(v),leaf(w))=b*w*(v-w);\n    Q_(n,check(w))=Q_(n,star)=Q_(star,n)=-b*u^2/4;\n    Q_(n,leaf(v))=-b*(v-u/2)^2.                       (5.1)\n\nIn particular both adjacent queried directions are nonpositive: following\na leaf has w=v, while at the preceding checkpoint the next leaf parameter\nis -d*w/M with d>0. Every E80 row is accounted for by this table. E80 means\nboth optimum orientations at every finite index, all checkpoint pairs,\nall leaf-to-checkpoint rows, adjacent checkpoint-to-leaf rows, and all\noutgoing actual-terminal rows. No leaf-to-leaf or remote checkpoint-to-\nleaf rows belonged to E80.\n\nThe growth estimate is included, rather than imported from the old draft.\nSince M=lambda-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],\n\n    Rreset-1=epsilon[2/(lambda(1+epsilon))-lambda*epsilon]/M\n              >epsilon/8=1/(32m).\n\nFor the strict bound, epsilon<1/8 and lambda<5/2 make the bracket exceed\n32/45-5/16>5/16, while M<5/2. For m>=16, induction gives\n2^(m-1)-1>=96m^2: the base is 32767>=24576 and the induction difference\n96m^2-192m-95 is positive. Hence N-1>32m(2m+1).\nThe binomial inequality (1+1/(32m))^(32m)>=2 yields\nRreset^(N-1)>2^(2m+1). Because M*c^(m-1)<2^(2m), we have t<-2.\n\nThis transcript is explicitly not interpolable. At pbad=n-2 the checkpoint\nparameter w=v_(N-2)=-M*t/beta>2M, whereas v_0=1/M. Consequently\n\n    Q_(pbad,0)=b*v_0*(w-v_0)>0, Q_(0,pbad)=0.        (5.2)\n\nLaw I omits this row. Law II includes it with positive weight. The new\nfailure below is insufficient paid action even though that violated row\nis now present. Neither (5.1) nor (5.2) is a lower bound on unrestricted W.\n\n### 5.1 A symbolic tail bound; no trajectory or recurrence was executed\n\nFor k=N-1-j, binary complementation gives exactly\n\n    |v_k/t|=Rreset^(-j)c^sbit(j), 1<=j<N.\n\nSince an integer with r binary ones is at least 2^r-1,\nc^(2sbit(j))<=(j+1)^4. Put rreset=Rreset^(-2). The previous lower bound\non Rreset gives 1-rreset>=1/(64m): with zeta=1/(32m)<1, use\n1-(1+zeta)^(-2)>=zeta/2.\nFor every integer d>=1,\nj^d<=d!*binom(j+d-1,d), and summing the geometric-series identity gives\nsum_(j>=1)j^d r^j<=d!/(1-r)^(d+1), 0<r<1. This identity follows by\nmultiplying d+1 geometric series and counting compositions, or by d\nsuccessive differentiations of their convergent series. Therefore\n\n    sum_(k<N-1)v_k^2/t^2 <=768(64m)^5=:Moment5,\n    sum_(p=2k+1 in Check)h_p*v_k^2/t^2\n                                  <=92160(64m)^7=:Moment7. (5.3)\n\nFor the second bound, v2(k+1)=v2(j), so\nh_p=1+beta*c^v2(j)<=1+j^2<=2j^2. Apply the d=6 inequality to\n2j^2(j+1)^4<=128j^6. For the first use (j+1)^4<=16j^4; the displayed\nconstant 768 is a harmless enlargement of 16*4!.\n\nAll coarse exponential comparisons used below follow from\n\n    2^(m-1)>2^24(64m)^7, m>=1536.                    (5.4)\n\nAt m=1536, m<2^11 makes the right side less than 2^143, whereas the\nleft side is 2^1535. The ratio of the left side to m^7 increases at each\ninteger increment, since (1+1/m)^7<sum_(j>=0)(7/m)^j<2 for m>=1536.\nThis proves (5.4) for all relevant m by induction. This is symbolic\nreasoning, not a generated arithmetic table or executed recurrence.\n\n## 6. Complete reset screen for both frozen laws\n\nOn (5.1), Law I has exact value\n\n    M_I(q)=q-2t^2/(b+2)-2b*(t-u/2)^2\n      +b*sum_(earlier leaves ell=2k)\n           [r81_ell*v_k*(t-v_k)-w81_ell*(t-v_k)^2]\n      +b*sum_(checkpoints p=2k+1)w81_p*t*(v_k-t).      (6.1)\n\nEvery selected original row appears here: outgoing optimum rows vanish,\nlast-leaf-to-checkpoint rows vanish, leaf pairs and both other orientations\nare evaluated by (5.1). For the frozen eta=1/(4D), w81_p<=2eta. Use\nv(t-v)<=t^2/4 and t(v-t)<=v^2/4, and sum r81_ell<2. Formula (5.3) and\n(5.4) give\n\n    last sum in (6.1) <=(b*eta/2)Moment5*t^2 <t^2/8.\n\nIndeed its coefficient is at most 96b(64m)^5/D, which is less than 1/8\nby b<3/2, D>2^m and (5.4). The leaf relay contribution is at most\nb*t^2/2 and its symmetric-pair part is nonpositive. Finally\n\n    2/(b+2)+2b*(b+1)^2/(b+2)^2-b/2\n       >4/7+7b/18 >121/126.\n\nHere (b+1)/(b+2)>2/3 and b>1. Thus\n\n    M_I(qtar)<qtar-(3/4)t^2<0.                       (6.2)\n\nThe inequality 121/126-1/8>3/4 and t^2>4 justify the displayed margin.\nThis reset conclusion is for the frozen eta law. The broader arbitrary-\ntheta contact family is excluded instead by (I.7), without a claim that\nevery such theta has the same sign on the reset transcript.\n\nFor Law II with kappa=1, the EXACT corresponding expression is\n\n    M_II(q)=q-2t^2/(b+2)-2b*(t-u/2)^2\n       +2b*sum_(p=2k+1)t_p*sum_(ell=2s<p)\n                         omega_(p,ell)*v_s*(v_k-v_s). (6.3)\n\nThe reverse leaf-to-checkpoint rows are zero, as are the source-to-\ncheckpoint and outgoing optimum rows. The displayed 2 is the actual\n1+kappa forward weight. Since v_s(v_k-v_s)<=v_k^2/4, the last sum is\nat most (b/2)sum_p t_p*v_k^2. The total checkpoint mass satisfies\nTc>=h_(2^(m-1)-1)>2^(m-1)/3. Equations (5.3)--(5.4) prove\n\n    sum_p t_p*v_k^2/t^2 <=Moment7/Tc<1/4.\n\nTherefore\n\n    M_II(qtar)<qtar-[2/(b+2)-b/8]*t^2\n                       <qtar-t^2/4<0.              (6.4)\n\nWe used 2/(b+2)-b/8>4/7-3/16>1/4 and qtar<1. The actual negative\nterminal-row cost was retained in (6.3) and only dropped for this upper\nbound. The included remote rows are too weak under this global degree\nnormalization. Their mere presence does not pay the required reset cost.\n\n## 7. The older two-gradient deep waves\n\nFor 2<=j<=m-3 set p=2^j-1, H=h_p, x_0=0, g_0=-H/b, g_p=1, and every\nother original gradient, including g_n, zero. The physical displacement\nis exactly zero. This is a formal full-space screen only.\n\nFor the frozen Law I, direct substitution in (I.3) gives\n\n    M_I=(r81_0+w81_0)H^2/b^2-(H-1)(r81_p+w81_p).           (7.1)\n\nBoth J(0,z) and J(p,z) equal m. Define, only for this calculation,\n\n    F(P)=(2-2eta/m)/(2S-b^2-P)+(2eta/m)/(S-b-P).\n\nThen r81_i+w81_i=h_i F(P81_i) for i=0,p, and F is strictly increasing.\nFor j>=3, H>4 and b/(b-1)<4; using F(P81_p)>F(0) in (7.1) gives\n\n    M_I<F(0)H[H/b-(H-1)]<0.                        (7.2)\n\nAt j=2, H=1+beta*c and P81_p=2b+1+beta<6. Now\nb-(b-1)H=1-beta^2>epsilon. Also\n\n    [F(P81_p)-F(0)]/F(0) <= P81_p/(S-b-P81_p).\n\nIt follows from (7.1) that M_I>0 if\n15/(S-8)<1/(6m), which follows from S>90m+8. This last inequality\nholds since S>2^m-1 and m>=1536 (the ratio 2^m/m increases for m>1,\nand the asserted comparison already holds at m=16).\nThus Law I passes exactly this j=2 screen and fails all j>=3 in its range.\n\nLaw II with kappa=1 passes every one of these old deep waves. Write\n\n    L_le=sum_(r in Check,r<=p)t_r*omega_(r,0),\n    L_gt=sum_(r in Check,r>p)t_r*omega_(r,0).\n\nIts exact value is\n\n    M_II=[(2-b)L_le+2L_gt]*H^2/b^2\n                 +2t_p-t_p*omega_(p,0)*(2b-3)*H/b>0. (7.3)\n\nTo derive it, the routed base contributes\n(L_le+L_gt)H^2/b^2+t_p-t_p*omega_(p,0)(b-1)H/b.\nFor a reverse cycle ending before p the interval displacement is b*g_0;\nfor one ending after p it is zero. The cycle at p contributes the\nremaining mixed term with coefficient b-2 and its checkpoint diagonal.\nAdding these gives (7.3). Its signs follow from b<3/2 and t_p>0.\nThe new equal-scale wave (II.6) still refutes its full matrix.\n\n## 8. The older H4 positive/negative block direction\n\nSet x_0=0, g_i=1 at i=0,1,2,3, g_i=-1 at i=8,9,10,11, and all other\noriginal gradients zero, including g_n. The two active four-query blocks\nhave identical physical mass, so displacement is zero. Put\n\n    H1=1+beta, H2=1+beta*c,\n    P2=b+H1, B4=2b+H1+H2, C4=b+H2.\n\nHere P2<7/2, B4<9, C4>4. All active original prefix masses P81_i are\nbelow 100, their total active step mass is 2B4<18 (in fact <17), and\nall J(i,z)=m.\n\nFor Law I put k0=(2-2eta/m)/(2S-b^2) and l0=(2eta/m)/(S-b).\nFreeze r81_i=k0 h_i,w81_i=l0 h_i only within this calculation. Zero physical\ndisplacement makes the exact frozen polynomial\n\n    (k0+l0)/2 * sum_active h_i(2-h_i).\n\nThe sum is twice\n2b(2-b)+(1+beta)(1-beta)+(1+beta*c)(1-beta*c), which is less than -1:\nthe first term is <=2, the second <1/4, and the third <1-(21/10)^2.\nThus the full sum is <-2 and the frozen value is <-(k0+l0).\n\nThe actual coefficients differ by at most\n|r81_i-k0 h_i|<=200 k0 h_i/S and\n|w81_i-l0 h_i|<=200 l0 h_i/S for each active i, since P81_i<100 and S>400.\nIn the polynomial (I.3) with g_z=x_0=0, the factor multiplying each\nr81_i or w81_i has absolute value at most 18: |g_i|<=1 and the total\nactive step mass is <17. Thus the total perturbation is at most\n61200(k0+l0)/S<(k0+l0)/2, since S>2^18. Consequently M_I<0.\n\nFor Law II use its exact routed expression (II.3), setting all values\nf_i=0 after their cancellation. Checkpoints p<=13 contribute at most\n4000/Tc in absolute value. To verify this bound, there are seven such\ncheckpoints, h_p<8, every formal position has absolute value <9,\neach finite row has absolute quadratic value <=20, and each optimum\nrow <=19/2. The bracket\nQ_(z,p)+sum omega[2Q_(p,ell)+Q_(ell,p)+Q_(ell,star)]\nhas absolute value <=70. Summing t_p<=56/Tc gives less than 4000/Tc.\n\nFor p>=15, g_p=0 and its ancestor height J(p,ell) is the SAME for all\nfour active leaves ell=0,2,8,10. Every such p is outside the cell\n{0,...,14} containing them. The full bracket equals\n\n    2*sum_active_leaves omega_(p,ell)*L_ell,\n    (L_0,L_2,L_8,L_10)=(1,1+P2,1-B4,1-B4+P2).\n\nSince raw_(p,ell)=(2p+1-ell)/[(p+1)J], its numerator before the positive\ndenominator (p+1)J Z_p is\n\n    N_p=8p-16+6P2+(16-4p)C4 <69-8p <=-3(p+1), p>=15.\n\nThis uses C4>4 and P2<7/2. Hence every such checkpoint contributes\nnegatively. At the whole root p0=2^(m-1)-1, J=m and Z_p0<=p0+1,\nso its contribution is at most\n\n    -6h_p0/[Tc*m*(p0+1)].\n\nThis one term already dominates 4000/Tc. Indeed\nh_p0/[m(p0+1)]>(7/16)(6/5)^(m-2)/m>4000.\nAt m=1536 use (6/5)^4>2 and 383 blocks of four powers, which give a\nlower bound exceeding 2^383 before division by m and multiplication by\n7/16. Thereafter (6/5)^(m-2)/m increases since m>5. Therefore M_II<0.\nThis is an all-depth analytical sign proof for the actual first-block\nembedding, not a finite numeric H4 experiment or a statement about all\nfour-block embeddings.\n\n## 9. What is excluded, omitted rows, and the remaining interface\n\nLaw I fails for every independent theta_i in [0,1] satisfying its\ndefinition, even though the exact optimum distribution and all remote\nfan weights are allowed to vary. The obstruction uses its deliberately\nbalanced contact equation (I.4). It does not exclude a different contact\nrelation, a different terminal distribution, or added independent rows.\nOne omitted potentially relevant row is Q_(n-2,0), explicitly positive\non the reset transcript. Adding it would define a different law, not\nrepair (I.2) within its parameters.\n\nLaw II fails for the literal LCA/position kernel (II.1), every common\nkappa>=0, and degrees t_p proportional to h_p. The necessary condition\n(II.8) applies to the specified routed support with arbitrary degrees\nand receiver probabilities, but is only one necessary two-coordinate\ntest. It is not an enlarged-support impossibility theorem. For example,\nthe omitted direct checkpoint pair Q_(p,r), with r,p from (II.6), and\nits reverse are not in (II.2). Either could alter that two-coordinate\nmatrix; neither is presumed to be a free PSD repair. Non-last leaf-to-\nleaf rows and nonuniform reverse-cycle amounts are also outside this law.\n\nThe concrete failure mechanisms are different. In Law I, exact contact\nbalance combines a large optimum charge at a long-step coordinate with\nnegative forward-pair diagonal costs. Increasing a common reverse-cycle coefficient\nscales the offending matrix. Its normalization by the whole checkpoint\nmass also underpays the reset transcript despite including its known\nviolated row. Passing the older two-gradient waves does not address this.\n\nAn unresolved full-function construction must pay the reset while\ncontrolling all such ancestor-crossing mixed terms, and must also prove\nthe complete remainder after the single charge (4.4) and actual terminal\nsquare (4.1). Merely requiring unknown blocks to be PSD restates the\nmissing theorem. We give no third coefficient law or automatic next round.\n\nNo positive primal candidate is asserted. Those restrictions were\nretained. All the new derivations below are one joint unverified attempt;\nnone is being adopted as an accepted intermediate fact.\n\n## 1. Outcome and precise scope\n\nWe first specify positive square factors on the ENTIRE original space,\nthen match them to original interpolation rows. The factorization is not\nan unknown PSD block, an arbitrary Cholesky factor, or a temporal quotient.\nEvery queried gradient, both child mean directions and their physical\ncheckpoint, every leaf, the common mode, the initial vector, and the\nunqueried terminal are retained.\n\nLaw I uses a complete physical-tree multiresolution factor and a terminal\ndistribution positive at every prefix sample and the optimum. Its literal\ncoefficient matching has a negative original multiplier: either the first\nquery to the last queried leaf, or the first query to the root checkpoint.\nThe failure holds for arbitrary positive weights on ALL the separate\ncontrasts, not just the two-parameter weight law displayed below. A stronger\nfirst-row invariant excludes every balanced-skew re-selection producing\nthis SAME factor and terminal distribution. Increasing q82 does not repair it.\n\nLaw II changes the matching by an explicit chronology-dependent skew term,\nchanges the terminal distribution to increasing weights on actual query\ntimes, and pays the resulting NONZERO initial/common derivative. Its full\nsquare identity contains the exact global charge tau^2/(2kappa). The two\nliteral original signs lambda_(0,z)>=0 and lambda_(z,z-2)>=0 already force\nq82>1/(128n). For EVERY assigned m>=1536, qtar82=17/(16D)<1/(128n).\nThus no member of this second literal law has both full PSD and all\nnonnegative multipliers at the target. An additional balanced-skew\nre-selection of Law II is outside this second exclusion.\n\nThe two results exclude two specified square/matching architectures.\nThey do not exclude the full dual cone, prove a lower bound on W, produce\nan interpolable primal witness, or resolve the original minimax question.\nThey do not invoke the previous contact-routed or LCA receiver arrays.\n\n## 2. Accepted inputs and the unchanged problem\n\nFor a predetermined nonnegative word h of length n, W_n(h) is the supremum\nof 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over every finite dimension,\nconvex differentiable L-smooth function with an attained minimizer x_star,\nand x_0!=x_star, with x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). L>0 is fixed.\nThus W=2R. The original question is whether\n\n    lim_(n->infinity) B_n inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n) W_n(h)=1.\n\nThe benchmark is the original ALL-integer-split recurrence\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt(u^2+6uv+v^2))/2,\n    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.\n\nAccepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the original interpolation\norientation, the independent-vector certificate interpretation, and the\nexact incoming-terminal-free completion. It removes NO prefix row.\nAccepted fact931b9653ce0f7201 supplies the same-word benchmark comparison\nB_n/D<47/50 and its CONDITIONAL implication from W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D).\nThat full-class upper hypothesis remains unproved. Its elementary mass\nformula is also available. No other mathematical fact is a premise here.\n\nFix an integer m>=1536. Throughout this report use the SAME enclosing m:\n\n    ell=1+sqrt(2), eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps,\n    c=ell*(1+eps), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, S=sum_(i<n)h_i, D=1+2S,\n    qtar82=17/(16D), z=n-1, r=2^(m-1)-1, b=1+a.\n\nHere z is the last QUERIED leaf, r is the whole-word checkpoint, and n\nis the ACTUAL terminal. All hierarchy symbols in this report are local.\nThe physical step at i is h_i=1+a*c^v2(i+1), where v2 is the exponent\nof 2 dividing a positive integer. Put Ppos_i=sum_(t<i)h_t and\nt_i=1+Ppos_i for 0<=i<n. The column t is an auxiliary clock, not time\nor a gradient. Write e for the all-ones column of length n and e_i for\nits coordinate unit columns. Superscript T denotes transpose.\nThe symbol 1_E is the indicator of a stated condition E, and 1_J is the\ncoordinate indicator column of an index set J. I denotes the n-by-n\nidentity matrix. A quadratic y^T K y for vector entries means\nsum_(i,j) K_ij <y_i,y_j>.\n\nThe following elementary bounds will suffice:\n\n    12/5<c<5/2, 7/8<beta<1, 1/3<a<1/2,\n    4/3<b<3/2, n>=15, S>n.\n\nIndeed sqrt(2)>7/5, and sqrt(2)<17/12 yields\nell<29/12; multiplying by 1+1/6144 still gives c<5/2.\nThen a>(7/8)/(5/2)=7/20>1/3; and a<1/ell<1/2.\nEvery step exceeds one. The displayed n bound follows from m>=1536.\n\n## 3. Full original coordinates; no projection or quotient\n\nNormalize L=1 and the optimum jet to x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Retain\nARBITRARY independent vectors x_0,g_0,...,g_n in a real Euclidean space.\nEvery position always means the polynomial\n\n    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t.\n\nFor distinct original labels define\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nActual legal jets obey Q_ij<=0. A certificate at level q82 requires\nlambda_ij>=0, finite divergence out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n), and PSD of\n\n    M(q82)=q82||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij\n\non ALL these independent vectors after the value cancellation.\n\nFor each queried i<n define y_i=x_i-g_i and delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.\nSet y_star=delta_star=0. Define the lower triangular matrix T82 by\n\n    T82_ii=1, T82_it=h_t for t<i, T82_it=0 for t>i.\n\nWrite g=(g_0,...,g_(n-1))^T and y=(y_0,...,y_(n-1))^T; in matrix\nproducts h also denotes its column. As vector-column identities,\n\n    y=e*x_0-T82g,  g=T82^(-1)(e*x_0-y),  T82e=t.             (3.1)\n\nT82 has diagonal one, so (x_0,y_0,...,y_(n-1),g_n) and the original\nvariables are in invertible correspondence. In particular y is an\narbitrary n-tuple once x_0 is fixed. No equal-gradient relation is imposed.\n\nWritten finite products define\n\n    pi_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t),\n    k_i=h_i*product_(i<t<n)(1-h_t), 0<=i<n.\n\nIn particular the inverse in (3.1) has the literal vector formula\n\n    g_i=pi_i*x_0-y_i\n             +sum_(j<i)h_j*product_(j<t<i)(1-h_t)*y_j.  (3.1a)\n\nAn empty product is one. Thus even the coordinate reconstruction is\ngiven by finite scalar coefficients, not an unspecified inverse solve.\n\nExpanding x_(i+1)=(1-h_i)x_i+h_i*y_i inductively, without evaluating a\nrecurrence on a machine, gives\n\n    x_n=pi_n*x_0+sum_(i<n)k_i*y_i,\n    sum_i k_i=1-pi_n, T82^T k=h.                       (3.2)\n\nFor the second identity, the coefficient sum on a suffix equals one\nminus the suffix product: expand each h_i=1-(1-h_i) and telescope.\nThus k_i+h_i sum_(j>i)k_j=h_i, proving the last identity.\n\nThere are n physical factors 1-h_i=-a*c^v2(i+1). Also\nsum_(i<n)v2(i+1)=sum_(j=1)^(m-1)(2^(m-j)-1)=n-m.\nSince n is odd, this proves the EXACT full-word product\n\n    pi_n=-s82,  s82=beta^n/c^m>0.                        (3.3)\n\nOnly an identity for the literal physical word is asserted; s82 is not\nbeing substituted for a worst-case contraction factor.\n\nChoose a parameter p82 with s82<p82<1, and a probability distribution\nalpha_i>=0 on queried indices with sum_i alpha_i=1-p82. The terminal\nweight at the optimum will be alpha_star=p82. Define\n\n    v=k-alpha,\n    w_i=p82+sum_(j<=i)alpha_j,\n    Rvec_i=h_i*w_i-alpha_i,\n    U82=sum_i Rvec_i=t^T v,\n    gamma=2*p82/(p82+s82), eta=(p82-s82)/(p82+s82).                  (3.4)\n\nThen\n\n    e^T v=p82+s82, T82^T v=Rvec, gamma=1+eta,\n    Rvec_i=h_i*(p82+sum_(j<i)alpha_j)+(h_i-1)alpha_i>0.\n\nThese identities follow from (3.2) and sum alpha=1-p82. In particular U82>0.\nThe exact terminal center in accepted1252b8823bb5a290 is\n\n    V=x_n-sum_i alpha_i*y_i=-s82*x_0+v^T y.            (3.5)\n\nEvery queried target and the optimum will have positive terminal weight\nin both laws. Neither law forces lambda_(n,n-1)=2.\n\n## 4. Signed coefficient matching and its exact full identity\n\nThis section gives a literal algebraic parameterization of the two laws,\nincluding the entries that FAIL nonnegativity. It is not a claim that a\npositive square factor necessarily has nonnegative row coefficients.\n\nLet K be a specified symmetric matrix with Ke=0. In the two laws K is\ngiven by explicit positive squares, never by an unknown PSD condition.\nLet A be a specified skew matrix, A^T=-A, and put d=Ae. Thus e^T d=0.\nDefine the fully specified matrices\n\n    Pmat=K+gamma*v*v^T,\n    Nmat=T82^T(Pmat+A).\n\nTo make clear that this is a finite coefficient formula, each entry is\n\n    Nmat_ij=K_ij+A_ij+h_i sum_(u>i)(K_uj+A_uj)\n                                      +gamma*Rvec_i*v_j.   (4.1)\n\nThe FULL original signed row array is\n\n    lambda_(n,star)=2*p82, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i, i<n,\n    lambda_(j,n)=0, j in {star,0,...,n-1},\n    lambda_(i,j)=-Nmat_ij, i!=j<n,\n    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i=(Nmat e)_i=2*p82*Rvec_i+(T82^T d)_i,\n    lambda_(star,i)=nu_i=(Nmat^T e)_i-2alpha_i\n                      =(K t)_i+gamma*U82*v_i-(A t)_i-2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(i,i)=0.                                      (4.2)\n\nThis lists every entry; no additional rows, diagonal multipliers, or\ncompletion freedom are presumed. Finite sums are definitions, not\nnumerical or recurrence executions.\n\nAll value-flow cancellations hold even before sign checking. At a prefix\nnode the total outgoing coefficient is Nmat_ii, and total incoming\nPREFIX coefficient is Nmat_ii-2alpha_i. Thus prefix divergence is\n2alpha_i. The incoming terminal edge has coefficient 2alpha_i, giving\nzero divergence at that original queried node. Terminal divergence is\n2*p82+2sum alpha_i=2. The optimum divergence is -2. All function values\ncancel in M(q82), without imposing any relation among them.\n\nDirect expansion yields Q_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i> for prefix\nindices, including star. Substituting (4.2), its surviving quadratic is\n-g^T Nmat y. Incoming-optimum rows have g_star=0; their value contribution\nis nevertheless present in the preceding flow calculation. From (3.1),\n\n    -g^T Nmat y\n       =-<x_0,e^T(Pmat+A)y>+y^T Pmat y,\n    e^T Pmat=2*p82*v^T, e^T A=-d^T.\n\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290, or direct expansion of the displayed outgoing\nterminal rows, now gives the ENTIRE original identity\n\n    M(q82)=||g_n+s82*x_0-v^T y||^2+R(q82),\n    R(q82)=y^T K y+eta||v^T y-(p82+s82)x_0||^2\n                         +(q82-p82^2)||x_0||^2+<x_0,d^T y>.  (4.3)\n\nFor direct checking, before completing the common square the prefix form is\n\n    (q82-s82^2)||x_0||^2+2(s82-p82)<x_0,v^T y>\n                              +eta||v^T y||^2+y^TKy+<x_0,d^Ty>.\n\nThe equality eta(p82+s82)=p82-s82 completes it to (4.3). This is an identity\non arbitrary original x_0,g_0,...,g_n, not merely a legal trajectory.\n\nThe common-gradient derivative is also explicit. Set every queried\ngradient to the same arbitrary vector G. Then y_i=x_0-t_i G and\nv^Ty=(p82+s82)x_0-U82 G. Therefore the FULL prefix restriction is\n\n    R_common=(q82-p82^2)||x_0||^2-<x_0,(d^T t)G>\n                                      +(t^TKt+eta U82^2)||G||^2. (4.4)\n\nIn particular an imbalance d is not free: it changes the derivative in\nG at G=0 by -(d^T t)x_0. Both optimum orientations are in (4.2), and\ntheir true contribution has not been replaced by a contact assumption.\n\n## 5. Law I: complete physical-tree squares, with separate boundaries\n\nUse uniform queried terminal weights\n\n    alpha_i=u=(1-p82)/n, alpha_star=p82.                    (I.1)\n\nFor each actual dyadic cell\n\n    I_(j,bidx)={bidx*2^j,...,(bidx+1)*2^j-2},\n    2<=j<=m, 0<=bidx<2^(m-j),\n\nlet Lcell and Rcell be its genuine left and right queried child sets,\neach of size l_j=2^(j-1)-1, and let rootcell be its actual checkpoint.\nDefine two coefficient columns of length n, zero outside the cell:\n\n    psi^-=(1_Lcell-1_Rcell)/sqrt(2l_j),\n    psi^+=(1_Lcell+1_Rcell-2l_j e_rootcell)\n                                      /sqrt(2l_j(2l_j+1)).  (I.2)\n\nThe first contrast retains the two child means independently. The second\nretains the checkpoint relative to both children. Each has sum zero and\nnorm one, and their inner product is zero. A descendant contrast has sum\nzero inside a child on which an ancestor contrast is constant. Thus all\nthese columns are mutually orthonormal. Inducting on a cell, its two\nchildren contribute 2(l_j-1) details, the displayed columns contribute\ntwo, and its common column contributes one, totaling 2l_j+1 coordinates.\nThe singleton H_1 cells need just their common column. Consequently\nthere are n-1 columns in (I.2), and together with e/sqrt(n) they form\nan orthonormal basis of the ENTIRE original queried-coordinate space.\nExplicitly, for a cell beginning at o=bidx*2^j, Lcell is\n{o,...,o+2^(j-1)-2}, rootcell=o+2^(j-1)-1, and Rcell is\n{o+2^(j-1),...,(bidx+1)*2^j-2}.\n\nThe literal two-parameter weight law is, for kappa>0 and theta>0,\n\n    omega^-_(j,bidx)=kappa*c^(-j),\n    omega^+_(j,bidx)=theta*kappa*c^(-j),\n    K=sum_cells[omega^- psi^- (psi^-)^T\n                                      +omega^+ psi^+ (psi^+)^T], A=0. (I.3)\n\nEvery square coefficient is explicitly positive at every depth. The\nstronger exclusion below permits independently arbitrary positive omega\nat every column, so it covers any retuning within this complete basis.\nThat extension does not insert an unknown PSD block.\n\nEquations (4.1)--(4.2) give EVERY original row coefficient. In this law\nmu_i=2*p82 Rvec_i>0; terminal weights are strictly positive. The incoming\noptimum coefficients are literally\n\n    nu_i=sum_cells,sign omega*psi_i*(sum_j psi_j t_j)\n                                                +gamma U82 v_i-2u.  (I.4)\n\nThey are signed matching coefficients, not silently assumed nonnegative.\nAn original finite coefficient will necessarily be negative regardless\nof whether these nu_i have acceptable signs.\n\nFor every q82>=p82^2 the complete factor is\n\n    M_I(q82)=||g_n+s82*x_0-v^T y||^2\n       +sum_cells,sign omega||sum_i psi_i y_i||^2\n       +eta||v^T y-(p82+s82)x_0||^2+(q82-p82^2)||x_0||^2.      (I.5)\n\nThis supplies all details and the common mode, since e^Tv=p82+s82>0 and\neta>0. With q82>p82^2 it is positive definite in all the original variables;\nat q82=p82^2 it has the stated explicit nonnegative factorization. No child\nPSD premise remains to prove. The only failed certificate requirement\nwill be the signs of the matched interpolation multipliers.\n\n### 5.1 An exact first forced sign, with the physical root retained\n\nLet omega^- and omega^+ now denote the two weights at the WHOLE word.\nOnly those two contrasts can contain both endpoints 0,z, or endpoint 0\nand root r. Since the child sizes are (n-1)/2,\n\n    K_0z=(-omega^-+omega^+/n)/(n-1),\n    K_0r=-omega^+/n.                                  (I.6)\n\nKe=0 implies (T82^T K)_0j=(1-b)K_0j=-a K_0j.\nHere Rvec_0=b*p82+a*u>0. The exact two signed entries are therefore\n\n    E_z=Nmat_0z=a*(omega^--omega^+/n)/(n-1)\n                                                   +gamma Rvec_0 v_z,\n    E_r=Nmat_0r=a*omega^+/n+gamma Rvec_0 v_r.           (I.7)\n\nThe last queried leaf has k_z=b, so v_z=b-u>1. The root has a suffix\nequal to the SAME-parameter H_(m-1). Write l=2^(m-1)-1. The product\ncalculation in (3.3), applied with that unchanged a,beta,c, gives\n\n    k_r=-(a+c^(-(m-1)))*beta^l,\n    v_r=k_r-u>-(a+1)-1/n>-2.                         (I.8)\n\nConsequently Bpair=v_r+(n-1)v_z>n-3>0, and (I.7) gives\n\n    E_r+(n-1)E_z=a*omega^-+gamma Rvec_0 Bpair>0.       (I.9)\n\nThis identifies a negative ORIGINAL multiplier for every parameter choice:\nif E_z>0, lambda_(0,z)=-E_z<0; if E_z<=0, (I.9) forces\nlambda_(0,r)=-E_r<0. All denominators and prefactors used here are positive.\nThe conclusion holds for every m>=1536, every s82<p82<1, every q82>=p82^2,\nevery kappa,theta>0, and even independently arbitrary positive detail\nweights. Descendant weights cannot alter (I.6). Raising q82 cannot alter\nthe sign because it changes only the last x_0 square in (I.5).\n\nThe excluded rows are actual remote queried-prefix rows. They are not\nincoming-terminal rows, omitted leaves, or fictitious child interfaces.\n\n### 5.2 All coefficient re-selections of this same factor also fail\n\nThere is a useful stronger scope statement. Keep the exact factor (I.5)\nand exact terminal distribution (I.1), but allow any other original prefix\nrow coefficients matching that polynomial. If their row matrix is Nnew,\nput Bnew=T82^(-T)Nnew-Pmat. Comparing y-y and x_0-y coefficients in (4.3)\nforces Bnew to be skew and e^T Bnew=0, equivalently Bnew e=0. Conversely\nevery such balanced-skew term leaves the same polynomial unchanged.\nThus this argument describes all coefficient re-selections of this fixed\nfactor, rather than assuming the symmetric matching is unique.\n\nFor any balanced-skew Bnew, the first diagonal change is\n\n    (T82^T Bnew)_00=(1-b)Bnew_00+b(e^T Bnew)_0=0,\n\nand the first row-sum change is zero. Hence the sum of its first-row\noff-diagonal N entries is invariant and equals\n\n    mu_0-Nmat_00\n       =a*K_00+gamma Rvec_0*(p82+s82-v_0)\n       =a*K_00+gamma Rvec_0*(p82+u-s82/a).                (I.10)\n\nHere k_0=b*s82/a, since pi_n=-s82=(1-b)*product_(1<=t<n)(1-h_t).\nThe last factor is strictly positive throughout the parameter domain.\nIndeed p82+(1-p82)/n is increasing in p82; at p82=s82,\n\n    n*(p82+u-s82/a)=1-s82*(n*(1/a-1)+1)>0.\n\nFor the strict last bound, a>1/3 gives n*(1/a-1)+1<2n+1<2^(m+1),\nand s82<c^(-m) gives its product with s82 less than 2*(5/6)^m<1.\nThe inequality (6/5)^4=1296/625>2 proves this for every m>=4.\nAlso K_00>0, because the complete basis has a nonzero contrast at 0\nand every detail weight is positive. Thus (I.10)>0.\n\nIf ALL original lambda_(0,j) were nonnegative, then all off-diagonal\nNnew_0j would be nonpositive and their sum could not be positive.\nThis contradiction excludes EVERY nonnegative coefficient matching of\nthis particular full square factor and its fixed terminal distribution.\nIt does not exclude a changed square factor or a changed terminal pool.\n\n## 6. Law II: chronological terminal pool and a paid skew correction\n\nChoose the positive increasing distribution on ACTUAL prefix indices\n\n    alpha_i=2(i+1)(1-p82)/[n(n+1)], 0<=i<n,\n    alpha_star=p82.                                      (II.1)\n\nIts sum is 1-p82 because sum_(i=0)^(n-1)(i+1)=n(n+1)/2.\nIts chronological cumulative is\n\n    w_i=p82+(1-p82)(i+1)(i+2)/[n(n+1)].\n\nThis is not a distribution on a handful of selected terminals. Every\ngenuine prefix sample participates; every physical leaf and checkpoint\nis still independent. In particular alpha_i<2/n.\n\nChoose kappa>0 and tau>=0, and set\n\n    K=kappa*(I-e e^T/n),\n    d=tau*(e_z-e_0),\n    A=(d e^T-e d^T)/n.                               (II.2)\n\nThen A^T=-A and Ae=d. This is a literal dense chronology correction,\nnot an unidentified circulation or an uncharged antisymmetric term.\nThe imbalance at the original first and last queried samples changes\nthe common-gradient derivative and the optimum coefficients.\n\nFor a completely scalar specification, put\n\n    Cclock_i=1+h_i*(n-1-i),\n    dtilde_0=a*tau,\n    dtilde_i=h_i*tau for 0<i<z,\n    dtilde_z=tau,\n    tbar=(sum_i t_i)/n.\n\nAll original finite coefficients come from the literal formula\n\n    Nmat_ij=kappa*(1_(i=j)+h_i*1_(j>i)-Cclock_i/n)\n            +gamma Rvec_i v_j+(dtilde_i-Cclock_i*d_j)/n,\n    lambda_(i,j)=-Nmat_ij for i!=j<n.                 (II.3)\n\nThe optimum coefficients and terminal coefficients are\n\n    mu_i=2*p82 Rvec_i+dtilde_i>0,\n    nu_i=kappa*(t_i-tbar)+gamma U82 v_i-d_i*tbar\n                                      +tau*Ppos_z/n-2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(n,star)=2*p82, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(j,n)=0, lambda_(i,i)=0.                   (II.4)\n\nThey follow from (4.2), using d^T t=tau*Ppos_z. This specifies ALL\nordered entries, including both optimum orientations. The nu entries\nremain signed matching expressions; their signs are not assumed.\nThe value-flow calculation of Section 4 applies without modification.\n\nWrite ybar=(sum_i y_i)/n. Completing ALL detail squares in (4.3) gives\nthe single global identity\n\n    M_II(q82)=||g_n+s82*x_0-v^T y||^2\n      +kappa*sum_(i<n)||y_i-ybar+(d_i/(2kappa))*x_0||^2\n      +eta||v^T y-(p82+s82)x_0||^2\n      +(q82-p82^2-tau^2/(2kappa))*||x_0||^2.             (II.5)\n\nSince sum d_i=0 and sum d_i^2=2tau^2, expanding the detail squares\nrecovers exactly kappa sum||y_i-ybar||^2+<x_0,d^T y> and the displayed\ncharge. There is no sum of independently minimized local Schur budgets.\nThe common-gradient restriction, in particular, is\n\n    R_II,common=(q82-p82^2)||x_0||^2-tau*Ppos_z*<x_0,G>\n            +[kappa sum_i(t_i-tbar)^2+eta U82^2]||G||^2.  (II.6)\n\nIt has the nonzero derivative -tau*Ppos_z*x_0 at G=0. Discarding that\nderivative would discard precisely the payment this construction makes.\n\nFull PSD of (II.5) is EQUIVALENT to\n\n    q82>=p82^2+tau^2/(2kappa).                            (II.7)\n\nSufficiency follows from the explicit squares. For necessity take any\nnonzero scalar x_0, set y_i-ybar=-(d_i/(2kappa))*x_0, and select the\ncommon value ybar so that v^T y=(p82+s82)x_0. This is possible because\nsum v_i=p82+s82>0. Finally set g_n=-s82*x_0+v^T y. These are arbitrary\nformal coordinates under the invertible map (3.1). They annihilate every\nother square. A negative last coefficient would therefore give an\nexplicit negative original-space direction. No interpolability is claimed.\nMore explicitly, take x_0=1,\ny_i=1+(v^T d)/(2kappa(p82+s82))-d_i/(2kappa), g_n=p82,\nand use (3.1a) for each queried g_i. The value of the whole polynomial is\nthen q82-p82^2-tau^2/(2kappa).\n\n### 6.1 Two literal original signs exceed the full charge budget\n\nLet j=z-2 be the preceding queried leaf. The final three physical steps\nare exactly (b,1+beta,b), so\n\n    k_z=b, k_j=b*a*beta.\n\nBy (II.1), alpha_i<2/n. Since n>=15 and b>4/3,\n\n    v_z>b-2/n>1,\n    v_j>b*a*beta-2/n\n         >7/18-2/15=23/90>1/4.                      (II.8)\n\nAlso gamma>1, Rvec_0>0. The final-row entry (II.3) becomes\n\n    Nmat_zj=(-kappa+tau)/n+gamma v_z v_j.             (II.9)\n\nIf lambda_(z,j)=-Nmat_zj>=0, then\n\n    kappa>=tau+n*gamma v_z v_j>tau+n/4>=n/4.          (II.10)\n\nThe first-to-last queried entry is exactly\n\n    Nmat_0z=a*kappa/n+gamma Rvec_0 v_z\n                                      -(b-2a/n)*tau. (II.11)\n\nThe factor b-2a/n is positive. If lambda_(0,z)>=0, (II.11) implies\n\n    b*tau>a*kappa/n,\n    tau>(a/b)*kappa/n>kappa/(4n).                    (II.12)\n\nThe last inequality follows from a=b-1 and b>4/3. The dropped terms\ngamma Rvec_0 v_z and 2a*tau/n were nonnegative, with the first strictly\npositive, so the strict comparison in (II.12) is justified.\n\nCombining (II.7), (II.10), and (II.12) gives\n\n    q82>=p82^2+tau^2/(2kappa)\n      >kappa/(32n^2)>1/(128n).                       (II.13)\n\nThis is a parameter-independent bound for this literal law. At any\nq82<=1/(128n), either its exact PSD condition fails, or one of the two\nexplicit original multipliers lambda_(z,z-2),lambda_(0,z) is negative.\nIf the PSD condition is imposed, an ordered sign audit is explicit:\ntest (II.9); if it is positive, lambda_(z,z-2)<0. If it is nonpositive,\n(II.10) holds and (II.7) forces tau<=sqrt(2kappa q82); at the forbidden\nlevel the inequalities used in (II.12) cannot hold, so (II.11)>0 and\nlambda_(0,z)<0. No claim is made that all the remaining signs pass.\n\nThis second failure is for the displayed A in (II.2) and complete literal\narray. A further balanced-skew term preserving this same factor may\nchange the two selected entries; unlike Section 5.2, no exclusion of\nthat enlarged second matching family is asserted.\n\n## 7. All-depth comparison with the target; nonempty factor domains\n\nThe hierarchy mass can be checked directly. Put\n\n    vword=a/(c-2)=beta/[c(c-2)], Aword=1+S.\n\nDefine A_j=1+sum H_j. The exact solution of\nA_(j+1)=2A_j+a*c^j, A_0=1 is\n\n    A_j=(1-vword)2^j+vword*c^j, 0<=j<=m.             (7.1)\n\nSubstitution proves the formula at every depth; it is not an executed\nrecurrence. Since c>ell>2 and ell(ell-2)=1, one has 0<vword<1.\nFurthermore\n\n    c(c-2)=1+2(ell+1)eps+ell^2 eps^2<1+7eps+6eps^2,\n    (1-8eps)(1+7eps+6eps^2)\n                       =1-eps-50eps^2-48eps^3<1-eps.\n\nThus vword>1-8eps=1-2/m>1/2. Equation (7.1) implies\n\n    Aword<c^m, D=2Aword-1>c^m-1,\n    D/n>(c/2)^m>(6/5)^m.                            (7.2)\n\nFor the middle strict ratio, (c^m-1)/(2^m-1)>c^m/2^m follows by\ncross multiplying and using c^m>2^m. The binomial expansion gives\n\n    (6/5)^8>1+8/5+28/25+56/125=521/125>4.\n\nRaising to the fourth power yields (6/5)^32>256. Therefore EVERY\nm>=1536 satisfies D>256n>136n, and hence\n\n    qtar82=17/(16D)<1/(128n).                          (7.3)\n\nThe factor domains are not vacuous. Because s82=beta^n/c^m and\nAword<c^m, s82*Aword<1. Thus pbase82=1/Aword belongs to (s82,1). Moreover\n\n    17*Aword^2-16*(2Aword-1)\n                               =16*(Aword-1)^2+Aword^2>0\n\nproves pbase82^2<qtar82. Law I therefore has strictly positive full factors at\nthe target, for example p82=pbase82 and kappa=theta=1. Their matched row signs\nfail by Section 5. For Law II, p82=pbase82 and tau=0 also give a full PSD\nfactor at qtar82 for every kappa>0, but the literal row signs fail. Choosing\ntau>0 attempts to repair signs; (II.13) proves its full common/initial\ncost exceeds the target before the two indicated signs can both pass.\n\nAll comparisons above are symbolic inequalities valid throughout the\nenclosing-m domain. No m-dependent child reset, dropped terminal step,\nnumerical asymptotic estimate, or replacement benchmark is used.\n\n## 8. Necessary screens, former attempts, and literature boundaries\n\nThe public recall distinguished the following earlier UNVERIFIED records:\n\n* 88f3db7b0a2bb506 is a PRIMAL Haar rank-one trajectory, rejected by an\n  original directed cycle. It supplies no dual factor or accepted lemma.\n* 8d6831f97708e3a9 concerns symmetric finite kernels and a normalized\n  tensor-mixture cone. Their directed-transport/first-query limitations\n  are awareness, not a premise in the new derivations.\n* 9da28a701244ea55 excludes specified contact-routed and LCA receiver\n  arrays. Their negative x_0=0 directions and reverse-cycle failure are\n  not being reused as a putative universal obstruction.\n* 0073a83e08ff2e21 exposes the E80 reset transcript and an omitted remote\n  prefix row. It is not an interpolable primal witness or a W lower bound.\n\nThe old H4 vector, deep zero-displacement waves, equal-scale checkpoint\npair, and the ENTIRE E80 reset transcript are all full original-vector\nassignments. In the stated PSD domains, (I.5) and (II.5) are nonnegative\non EVERY assignment, so those necessary quadratic screens are covered\nwithout numerical replay. This is stronger than checking their values\none by one, but it is NOT a certificate: the original row signs fail.\nFor E80 in particular, positivity of our square on its formal transcript\ndoes not permit using a signed interpolation combination as an upper\nbound. No legality or terminal payoff is inferred from that screen.\n\nThe exact primary source Altschuler--Parrilo, \"Acceleration by Stepsize\nHedging II: Silver Stepsize Schedule for Smooth Convex Optimization\",\narXiv:2309.16530, Sections 3.1--3.2, was read as background. Its proof\norganizes gluing, rank-one corrections, and sparse corrections; the\ncomponent corrections need not all be positive, while final multipliers\nmust be. Its residual orientation is Qpaper_ij=-2Q_(j,i) here, and its\nword is the undeformed Silver word. No theorem from it is imported for\nour altered word or normalized W bound. The coefficient checks above\nare self-contained; this is a proof-pattern comparison only.\n\nThe Matlas query returned unrelated PL, minimax-extragradient, and\nstochastic-subgradient results; none was used. Web retrieval located\nthe exact source just named. The PDF was downloaded into this worker's\ndownloads directory. Local extraction failed with exit127,\n\"pdftotext: command not found\"; pypdf/PyPDF2/fitz were absent. No parser\nwas installed. An arXiv HTML endpoint returned404; the exact PDF text\nand proof were then read through the web tool. No successful LOCAL text\nextraction is claimed. No external theorem needs external_refs because\nthere is no submission and no external result in the proof dependencies.\n\n## 9. Failure synthesis and the remaining interface\n\nLaw I has an explicit full PSD factor but no nonnegative original-row\nrepresentation with its fixed terminal pool. The first-row invariant\nsurvives every balanced-skew re-selection preserving that exact factor.\nThis is an original coefficient obstruction, not a missing full-space\nterm, an untested child block, or failure of a restricted kernel test.\n\nLaw II allows a nonzero skew divergence and accounts for its genuine\ncommon-gradient derivative. Its correction costs tau^2/(2kappa) ONCE,\nglobally. The literal correction can make a first-query edge less\npositive in Nmat, but the last-to-previous-leaf edge demands a larger\ndetail coefficient. The two demands force the stated excessive charge.\nThe obstruction concerns this one skew pattern and terminal distribution,\nnot arbitrary chronological corrections or further coefficient re-selection.\n\nThe still-needed mechanism is an explicit full factor AND a nonnegative\noriginal-row matching at q82<=17/(16D) for every SAME-m m>=1536. A changed\nterminal distribution, a nonisotropic chronology-dependent detail system,\nor a more general skew correction with an analytically bounded aggregate\ncharge lies outside these exclusions. Those are open interfaces, not\nadditional laws attempted under this two-law allocation, and no unknown\nPSD or sign-feasibility problem is offered as their solution.\n\nThe original all-word/all-horizon minimax Goal remains OPEN.
