All those\ncounts remain zero. All scalar inequalities below are textual mathematics.\n\n## 1. Outcome and exact scope\n\nThe exact suffix-product scale is retained in BOTH the nonisotropic detail\nmetric and the actual terminal distribution. Every original queried gradient,\nthe actual unqueried terminal gradient, and the initial vector remain\nindependent. The two explicit laws use the same weighted full square and\ndistributed imbalance, but genuinely different original coefficient arrays.\n\nLaw A uses the canonical weighted skew for that imbalance. Throughout its\nentire domain, its incoming optimum multiplier at original query1 is\nnegative. Independently, making its first-to-last queried multiplier\nnonnegative forces its exact global square charge above the assigned\nqtar=17/(16D). This quantifies why paying through an exponentially tiny\ninitial tail weight fails in this particular matching.\n\nLaw B adds an EXPLICIT BALANCED skew transport, with no sign-feasibility\noracle. It equalizes the entire first row, and can make all its finite\nmultipliers nonnegative while maintaining the full PSD factor at qtar.\nNevertheless, for EVERY member of Law B, either the original multiplier\nfrom the last queried leaf to the preceding queried leaf is negative, or\nthe original incoming optimum multiplier at query1 is negative. This is\nan exhaustive obstruction for this displayed balanced correction, including\narbitrary positive metric scale and arbitrary nonnegative imbalance scale.\n\nNeither exclusion is an impossibility theorem for all balanced corrections,\nall weighted variances, all terminal probabilities, or the full dual cone.\nNo primal interpolability or lower bound on W is inferred from a formal\nnegative direction or a negative signed multiplier. No full upper bound on\nW, benchmark improvement, or resolution of the original problem is obtained.\n\n## 2. Original problem and accepted inputs\n\nFor a predetermined nonnegative word h of length n and L>0, W_n(h) denotes\nthe supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite\nEuclidean dimension, convex differentiable L-smooth function with an\nattained minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, under the original recursion\nx_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R. The original question concerns\nwhether W_n_star/U_n^ZJ tends to1, where W_n_star=inf_(h>=0)W_n(h) and\nU_n^ZJ=1/B_n. The original benchmark retains EVERY integer split:\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt(u^2+6uv+v^2))/2,\n    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)).\n\nAccepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the original interpolation\norientation, full independent-vector certificate interpretation, and exact\nincoming-terminal-free reduction. It deletes NO queried-prefix row.\nAccepted fact931b9653ce0f7201 supplies the same-enclosing-m word below,\nits mass identity and the scalar comparison B_n/D<47/50. Its implication\nfrom W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D) to a strict benchmark improvement is CONDITIONAL;\nthat full-class upper hypothesis is still missing. These are the only\naccepted mathematical inputs used here. Historical draft6e66efffac0795f1\nis recall only; its coordinate and row identities are rederived below.\n\nFix ANY integer m>=1536. All children use this SAME enclosing m:\n\n    ell=1+sqrt(2), eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps,\n    c=ell(1+eps), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, z=n-1,\n    S=sum_(i<n)h_i, Aword=1+S, D=1+2S=2Aword-1,\n    p=1/Aword, qtar=17/(16D), b=1+a.\n\nThe symbols p and q in this report mean p83 and q83; neither is a benchmark\nexponent or a primal derivative. The letter B without a subscript below is\na skew matrix, not the benchmark B_n. All other hierarchy notation is local.\nThe actual physical step is h_i=1+a*c^v2(i+1), where v2(j) is the exponent\nof2 dividing the positive integer j. The final three steps are\n(b,1+beta,b). The first and last queried steps equal b; index n is the\nACTUAL terminal, distinct from z.\n\nThe elementary bounds needed below are\n\n    12/5<c<5/2, 7/8<beta<1, 1/3<a<1/2, 4/3<b<3/2.       (2.1)\n\nFor example sqrt(2)>7/5 and sqrt(2)<17/12; hence ell>12/5 and\nell<29/12, and (29/12)(1+1/6144)<5/2. Also beta>7/8,\na>(7/8)/(5/2)>1/3, and a<1/ell<1/2.\nThe accepted mass formula is\n\n    Aword=(1-vword)2^m+vword*c^m,\n    vword=beta/[c(c-2)] in (0,1).\n\nIndeed c>ell>2 and ell(ell-2)=1 give c(c-2)>1. Consequently\n\n    2^m<Aword<c^m, 0<p<2^(-m)<1/128.                  (2.2)\n\nEvery h_i>1, consistently with these strict mass inequalities.\n\n## 3. Exact physical suffix products and the selected terminal law\n\nWrite sbit(L) for the binary digit sum of a nonnegative integer L, including\nsbit(0)=0. For 0<=L<=n define the product of the FINAL L actual factors:\n\n    r_L=product_(t=n-L)^(n-1)(h_t-1), r_0=1.\n\nThe index j counted backward from the final physical step has\nh_(n-j)-1=a*c^v2(2^m-j)=a*c^v2(j), for 1<=j<2^m. The valuation equality\nfollows by factoring the exact power of2 from j: the remaining difference\nfrom2^m is odd. For any L, sum_(j=1)^L v2(j)=sum_(t>=1)floor(L/2^t).\nWriting L in binary and summing each digit's contributions gives\nsum_(t>=1)floor(L/2^t)=L-sbit(L). Therefore the EXACT suffix identity is\n\n    r_L=beta^L*c^(-sbit(L)), 0<=L<=n.                 (3.1)\n\nNo product or recurrence has been numerically evaluated. In particular\ns:=r_n=beta^n/c^m, and the signed full-word product is -s since n is odd.\nDefine the actual terminal coefficient and its absolute scale by\n\n    k_i=h_i product_(i<t<n)(1-h_t),\n    u_i=|k_i|, U=sum_(i<n)u_i, rho_i=u_i/U.\n\nIf L=n-1-i, adding the next backward factor shows\n\n    u_i=r_L+r_(L+1), k_i=(-1)^L u_i=(-1)^i u_i.       (3.2)\n\nThus all u_i are strictly positive. The last three are\n\n    u_z=b, u_(z-1)=a(1+beta), u_(z-2)=b*a*beta,\n    u_0=(b/a)s.                                      (3.3)\n\nExpanding a product over the m binary digits gives the finite identity\n\n    Z=sum_(L=0)^n r_L=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1+beta^(2^j)/c),\n    U=2Z-1-s <2(3/2)^m.                              (3.4)\n\nThe last inequality uses beta<1 and c>2. The three distinct last terms\nin (3.3), with (2.1), give\n\n    U>4/3+5/8+7/18=169/72>7/3.                       (3.5)\n\nBoth laws use these SAME explicit terminal probabilities:\n\n    alpha_star=p, alpha_i=(1-p)rho_i=delta*u_i,\n    delta=(1-p)/U, rplus=1-delta, rminus=1+delta,\n    v=k-alpha.                                       (3.6)\n\nThey sum to1 and are strictly positive on every original queried sample\nand the optimum. They are probabilities on real original rows, not on a\nselected child interface. Since U>7/3, one has 0<delta<3/7, rplus>1/2,\nand 1<rminus<3/2. Precisely\n\n    v_i=rplus*u_i for i even,\n    v_i=-rminus*u_i for i odd.                        (3.7)\n\nSeveral uniform small-initial-weight bounds will be used. The binomial\nexpansion implies n=2^m-1>m(m-1)/2>128m for m>=1536. Also\n(1-eps)^(-n)>(1+eps)^n>=1+n*eps, so beta^n<1/33. From Aword<c^m,\ns/p=beta^n*Aword/c^m<beta^n. Because b/a=1+1/a<4,\n\n    s<p/33, u_0<4p/33<p/8,\n    alpha_0<p/8, rho_0<3p/56<1/10.                  (3.8)\n\nThese are symbolic inequalities uniform in all enclosing m in the task.\n\n## 4. Full original coordinates and the complete signed matching\n\nNormalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Keep arbitrary independent\nvectors x_0,g_0,...,g_n, with every original position defined by\n\n    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t.\n\nThe residual orientation is\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nLegal actual jets obey Q_ij<=0. For i<n let y_i=x_i-g_i and\nDelta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2, with the corresponding star quantities zero.\nLet e be the all-ones column of length n, e_i its coordinate columns, and\nT=T82 the lower triangular matrix T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, zero for t>i.\nFor vector columns g=(g_0,...,g_(n-1)) and y=(y_0,...,y_(n-1)),\n\n    y=e*x_0-Tg, g=T^(-1)(e*x_0-y).                    (4.1)\n\nThis is invertible on the ENTIRE formal space. Explicitly, if\npi_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t), then\n\n    g_i=pi_i*x_0-y_i\n          +sum_(j<i)h_j product_(j<t<i)(1-h_t)*y_j.  (4.2)\n\nSubstitution into x_(i+1)=(1-h_i)x_i+h_i*y_i proves\n\n    x_n=-s*x_0+k^T y, e^T k=1+s, T^T k=h.           (4.3)\n\nFor the latter identity, h_j=1-(1-h_j) telescopes on each suffix:\nk_i+h_i sum_(j>i)k_j=h_i. In particular e^T v=p+s.\nThe cumulative terminal probabilities, distinct from detail weights below,\nare Cprob_i=p+sum_(j<=i)alpha_j. Define\n\n    R_i=h_i*Cprob_i-alpha_i,\n    t_i=1+sum_(j<i)h_j, t=Te,\n    Uclock=sum_i R_i=t^T v,\n    gamma=2p/(p+s), eta=(p-s)/(p+s).\n\nThen T^T v=R and\nR_i=h_i(p+sum_(j<i)alpha_j)+(h_i-1)alpha_i>0. Thus Uclock>0,\n1<gamma<2, eta>0 and gamma=1+eta. In particular\n\n    R_0=bp+a*alpha_0, H=p+s-v_0=p+alpha_0-s/a,\n    bp<R_0<25p/16, 7p/8<H<9p/8.                     (4.4)\n\nThe lower bound on H follows from H=p+s-rplus*u_0>p-u_0; its upper\nbound follows from the second expression in (4.4).\nThe actual terminal center is V=-s*x_0+v^T y.\n\nFor the moment let w_i>0 be arbitrary DETAIL weights, Ww=sum_i w_i,\nand let d be any real column with e^T d=0. Define\n\n    K=diag(w)-w*w^T/Ww,\n    A=(d*w^T-w*d^T)/Ww+B, B^T=-B, Be=0,\n    P=K+gamma*v*v^T, N=T^T(P+A).                     (4.5)\n\nThus Ke=0, A^T=-A and Ae=d. The matrices are instantiated explicitly in\nSections6 and7; an arbitrary sign-feasible B is NOT a proposed result.\nEvery scalar entry is\n\n    N_ij=K_ij+A_ij+h_i sum_(u>i)(K_uj+A_uj)\n                                      +gamma*R_i*v_j. (4.6)\n\nThe entire ORIGINAL SIGNED coefficient array is\n\n    lambda_(n,star)=2p, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(j,n)=0 for j in {star,0,...,n-1},\n    lambda_(i,j)=-N_ij for i!=j<n,\n    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i=(Ne)_i=2p*R_i+(T^T d)_i,\n    lambda_(star,i)=nu_i=(N^T e)_i-2alpha_i\n                      =(Kt)_i+gamma*Uclock*v_i-(At)_i-2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(i,i)=0.                                  (4.7)\n\nSigns are not presumed. This lists every original coefficient, including\nboth optimum orientations and the actual outgoing terminal distribution.\nFor each prefix index i, the outgoing sum is N_ii, while the incoming\nPREFIX sum is N_ii-2alpha_i. The incoming terminal coefficient2alpha_i\ntherefore gives zero total divergence at i. Terminal divergence is2;\noptimum divergence is-2. Consequently all original sampled values cancel\nin M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij.\n\nTo check the quadratic directly, Q_ij=Delta_i-Delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>\nfor prefix indices, including star. The prefix value terms cancel the\n-2sum alpha_i Delta_i from the outgoing terminal expansion. The remaining\nprefix term is -g^T N y. Since N=T^T(P+A), this equals\n\n    -<x_0,e^T(P+A)y>+y^T P y,\n    e^T P=2p*v^T, e^T A=-d^T.\n\nThe terminal expansion contributes ||g_n-V||^2-||V||^2. Completing the\ncommon square gives the full identity, on arbitrary original vectors,\n\n    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2+y^T K y\n       +eta||v^T y-(p+s)x_0||^2\n       +(q-p^2)||x_0||^2+<x_0,d^T y>.                (4.8)\n\nFor an expansion check, the unsquared prefix terms are\n(q-s^2)||x_0||^2+2(s-p)<x_0,v^T y>+eta||v^T y||^2\n+y^T K y+<x_0,d^T y>. The relation eta(p+s)=p-s proves (4.8).\n\n## 5. Weighted completion, the real common derivative, and a row invariant\n\nLet ybar_w=sum_i w_i*y_i/Ww. Since sum_i d_i=0, expanding the squares gives\n\n    sum_i w_i||y_i-ybar_w+(d_i/(2w_i))*x_0||^2\n    =y^T K y+<x_0,d^T y>+J||x_0||^2,\n    J=(1/4)sum_i d_i^2/w_i.                          (5.1)\n\nThus the COMPLETE factor is\n\n    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2\n       +sum_i w_i||y_i-ybar_w+(d_i/(2w_i))*x_0||^2\n       +eta||v^T y-(p+s)x_0||^2+(q-p^2-J)||x_0||^2.  (5.2)\n\nThe charge J is paid ONCE globally. No child gradient, common mode, leaf,\ncheckpoint, terminal square or initial cross derivative has been removed.\nThe detail form has kernel exactly the constant y columns, because every\nw_i>0. The common square controls that remaining direction since e^T v>0.\n\nFull PSD in (5.2) is EQUIVALENT to q>=p^2+J. To prove necessity take scalar\nx_0=1, set c0=1+[sum_i v_i*d_i/(2w_i)]/(p+s), y_i=c0-d_i/(2w_i),\nand g_n=p. The weighted mean of y is c0 because sum d_i=0. All detail,\ncommon and terminal squares vanish. Reconstruct each original g_i by\n(4.2). The value of M is exactly q-p^2-J. This is a formal full-space\nnegative direction if that quantity is negative; it is NOT an actual\ninterpolable primal trajectory.\n\nSetting every original queried gradient equal to an arbitrary vector G\ninstead gives y_i=x_0-t_iG. Directly in (4.8),\n\n    R_common=(q-p^2)||x_0||^2-(d^T t)<x_0,G>\n                      +(t^T Kt+eta*Uclock^2)||G||^2. (5.3)\n\nThe genuine nonzero initial/common derivative is -(d^T t)x_0. A balanced\nB changes neither this derivative nor the full square polynomial.\n\nNow derive the original FIRST-ROW off-diagonal sum including d_0.\nThe first column of T is (1,b,...,b)^T. Since K has column sums zero,\n(T^T K)_00=-a*K_00. Skewness gives A_00=0, and e^T A=-d^T gives\n(T^T A)_00=-b*d_0. Meanwhile (T^T d)_0=-a*d_0. Subtracting the diagonal\nfrom the row sum in (4.7) yields\n\n    sum_(j>0) N_0j\n       =a*K_00+gamma*R_0*(p+s-v_0)+d_0\n       =a*K_00+gamma*R_0*H+d_0.                     (5.4)\n\nEvery balanced B leaves this sum invariant. Nonnegative finite first-row\nmultipliers require the right side to be <=0. A balanced correction can\nredistribute that sum among entries; it cannot change the sum itself.\n\nThere is also an exact inverse-metric price for this invariant. Weighted\nCauchy--Schwarz on i>0 gives\nsum_(i>0)d_i^2/w_i >=d_0^2/(Ww-w_0), since sum_(i>0)d_i=-d_0.\nConsequently\n\n    J>=d_0^2/(4*K_00), K_00=w_0(1-w_0/Ww).          (5.5)\n\nEquality holds precisely when d_i=-d_0*w_i/(Ww-w_0) for every i>0.\nBecause H>0, (5.4)--(5.5) imply for any nonnegative first row\n\n    J>=(a*K_00+gamma*R_0*H)^2/(4*K_00)\n      >=a*gamma*R_0*H.                               (5.6)\n\nThe final inequality expands the nonnegative square\n(a*K_00-gamma*R_0*H)^2. This is a necessary first-row screen, NOT a\nsufficient certificate condition. The two selected laws now instantiate\nall weights and skew matrices rather than leaving the screen unsolved.\n\n## 6. Law A: exact tail metric with minimum-price distributed imbalance\n\nFor EVERY kappa>0 and tau>=0 choose\n\n    w_i=kappa*u_i, Ww=kappa*U,\n    d=tau*(rho-e_0),\n    A_A=tau*(rho*e_0^T-e_0*rho^T), B_A=0.             (6.1)\n\nHere A_A=(d*w^T-w*d^T)/Ww exactly, and A_A e=d. The imbalance is\nnegative at0 and positive at EVERY other actual queried index, not just\nat one endpoint. It attains equality in (5.5). The metric is nonisotropic\nbecause u_0<p/8 whereas u_z=b>4/3. The terminal probabilities remain (3.6).\n\nThe exact global charge and common derivative are\n\n    J_A=tau^2*(1-rho_0)/(4*kappa*u_0),\n    d^T t=tau*(tbar-1), tbar=sum_i rho_i*t_i.          (6.2)\n\nThus (5.2) is a fully specified factor iff q>=p^2+J_A.\nFor a literal scalar description of EVERY finite entry put\nF_i=u_i+h_i sum_(j>i)u_j. Then\n\n    N^A_ij=kappa*(u_i*1_(i=j)+h_i*u_j*1_(j>i)-F_i*rho_j)\n          +gamma*R_i*v_j\n          +tau*((F_i/U)*1_(j=0)-1_(i=0)*rho_j).      (6.3)\n\nTogether with (4.7), this is the complete array. Both optimum orientations\ncan also be written explicitly:\n\n    mu_0=2p*R_0+a*tau*(1-rho_0)>0,\n    mu_i=2p*R_i+tau*F_i/U>0 for i>0,\n    nu^A_i=kappa*u_i*(t_i-tbar)+gamma*Uclock*v_i\n                         -tau*(rho_i-1_(i=0)*tbar)-2alpha_i. (6.4)\n\nAll terminal outgoing signs and all outgoing optimum signs pass. No sign\nis silently assigned to nu^A or an off-diagonal -N^A.\n\n### 6.1 A first-row charge obstruction at the assigned target\n\nSince v_z=rplus*u_z, the actual first-to-last queried entry is\n\n    N^A_0z=rho_z*(a*kappa*u_0-tau)\n                                +gamma*R_0*rplus*u_z. (6.5)\n\nIf lambda_(0,z)=-N^A_0z>=0, positivity of rho_z gives\n\n    tau>=a*kappa*u_0+gamma*R_0*(U-1+p).               (6.6)\n\nApply (a*x+C)^2/(4x)>=a*C for x=kappa*u_0>0 and\nC=gamma*R_0*(U-1+p)>0. Equations (6.2) and (6.6) imply\n\n    J_A>=a*gamma*R_0*(U-1+p)*(1-rho_0)\n       >(4/9)*p*(4/3)*(9/10)=8p/15.                (6.7)\n\nThe strict lower bounds use a*b>4/9, gamma>1, R_0>bp, U>7/3 and\nrho_0<1/10. On the other hand Aword>128 gives\n\n    qtar/p=17*Aword/[16*(2Aword-1)]<8/15,             (6.8)\n\nbecause this inequality is equivalent to Aword>128. Thus no member of\nLaw A has both full PSD and lambda_(0,z)>=0 at q<=qtar. This argument\nuses the exact SINGLE global charge and no local Schur-budget summation.\n\n### 6.2 A stronger literal incoming-optimum failure for every q\n\nThe weighted clock is uniformly large. By (3.3)--(3.5), t_z>n and\nn>2^(m-1), so\n\n    tbar>=rho_z*t_z> b*n/[2*(3/2)^m]\n                     >(1/3)*(4/3)^m>27.             (6.9)\n\nFor the last inequality m>=16 and (4/3)^4=256/81>3, so (4/3)^16>81.\nMeanwhile t_1=1+b<5/2<tbar/2. Query1 has v_1=-rminus*u_1<0.\nIn (6.4) its metric term is strictly negative, its rank term is strictly\nnegative, its skew term is nonpositive, and -2alpha_1 is strictly negative.\nHence\n\n    nu^A_1=lambda_(star,1)<0                         (6.10)\n\nfor EVERY m>=1536, kappa>0, tau>=0 and every real q. This excludes\nLaw A as an original-row certificate even at larger q. It does not exclude\na balanced re-selection, which can change nu_1 and (6.5). Law B performs\none such correction explicitly; it is not dismissed by (6.10).\n\n## 7. Law B: an explicit balanced transport that repairs the entire first row\n\nKeep the SAME w,d,alpha,p and arbitrary kappa>0,tau>=0 as in Law A.\nDefine the probability column on noninitial queried samples\n\n    r_0^pool=0, r_i^pool=u_i/(U-u_0) for i>0,\n    chi=e_0-r^pool,\n    xi=gamma*R_0/a,\n    bvec_0=0, bvec_i=xi*(v_i-H*r_i^pool) for i>0,\n    B_B=chi*bvec^T-bvec*chi^T,\n    A_B=A_A+B_B.                                     (7.1)\n\nThe r^pool column is distinct from the suffix product r_L. Its denominator\nis positive, and its entries sum to1. Since sum_(i>0)v_i=H, both chi and\nbvec have sum zero. Therefore B_B is skew AND B_B e=0. In particular\nA_B e=d, and the entire factor (5.2), charge (6.2), and real derivative\n(5.3) are unchanged. There is no hidden skew unknown in this law.\n\nFor complete scalar coefficients define\nC_i=chi_i+h_i sum_(j>i)chi_j and\nE_i=bvec_i+h_i sum_(j>i)bvec_j. Then\n\n    N^B_ij=N^A_ij+C_i*bvec_j-E_i*chi_j.              (7.2)\n\nEquations (4.7), (6.3), (7.1)--(7.2) specify EVERY original coefficient.\nThe outgoing optimum coefficients are still the strictly positive mu_i\nin (6.4). The incoming optimum coefficients are\n\n    nu^B_i=nu^A_i-chi_i*(bvec^T t)+bvec_i*(chi^T t).  (7.3)\n\nNo positivity of these incoming coefficients is assumed.\n\n### 7.1 The entire first row is explicitly equalized\n\nFor j>0, B_B,0j=bvec_j. Because B_B has zero column sums,\n(T^T B_B)_0j=-a*bvec_j. Substituting (7.1) in (6.3) yields\n\n    N^B_0j=r_j^pool*S0,\n    S0=(a*kappa*u_0-tau)*(1-rho_0)+gamma*R_0*H.      (7.4)\n\nIn particular ALL original finite first-row multipliers are nonnegative\niff S0<=0, equivalently\n\n    tau>=a*kappa*u_0+gamma*R_0*H/(1-rho_0).           (7.5)\n\nThis realizes the invariant (5.4), rather than assuming it can be repaired.\nIt changes the demanding Law A quantity U-1+p in (6.6) to the much smaller\nH/(1-rho_0). Thus the Law A charge obstruction does NOT transfer to Law B.\n\nThe simultaneous first-row and full-PSD conditions at qtar are nonvacuous.\nWithin this same two-parameter law choose the explicit member\n\n    kappa_0=gamma*R_0*H/[a*u_0*(1-rho_0)],\n    tau_0=2*gamma*R_0*H/(1-rho_0).                    (7.6)\n\nThese are positive. Equation (7.4) gives N^B_0j=0 for every j>0, and\nJ_A=a*gamma*R_0*H. From (2.1), gamma<2 and (4.4),\n\n    J_A< (25p/16)*(9p/8)=225p^2/128<2p^2.\n\nConsequently p^2+J_A<3p^2<p/2<qtar, because p<1/128 and\nqtar/p=17*Aword/[16*(2Aword-1)]>17/32>1/2. This is a strictly positive\nfull factor at the target with all finite first-row multipliers zero,\npositive terminal probabilities, and positive outgoing optimum multipliers.\nIt remains a FAILED certificate because the complete array still has a\nnegative original coefficient, as the next subsection proves.\n\n### 7.2 Last-to-previous-leaf versus incoming optimum1: exhaustive sign conflict\n\nLet j=z-2, the genuine preceding queried leaf. Both z and j are even.\nFor i,j>0 the balanced correction has the explicit entry\n\n    B_B,ij=xi*(v_i*r_j^pool-r_i^pool*v_j).            (7.7)\n\nBy (3.7), v_z/u_z=v_j/u_j=rplus. Therefore B_B,zj=0. Also\nA_A,zj=0. The last row of T^T is the last coordinate row, so exactly\n\n    N^B_zj=u_z*u_j*(-kappa/U+gamma*rplus^2).          (7.8)\n\nIf kappa<gamma*U*rplus^2, this entry is positive and the ORIGINAL\nlambda_(z,z-2) is negative. Otherwise its sign passes and necessarily\n\n    kappa>=gamma*U*rplus^2>7/12.                     (7.9)\n\nThe strict lower bound follows from gamma>1, U>7/3 and rplus>1/2.\nWe now prove that throughout this remaining domain nu^B_1 is negative.\n\nPut tpool=sum_i r_i^pool*t_i=(tbar-rho_0)/(1-rho_0). Then\nbvec^T t=xi*(Uclock-v_0-H*tpool), chi^T t=1-tpool.\nFor i>0, using (7.3) gives the exact expression\n\n    nu^B_i=kappa*u_i*(t_i-tbar)\n       +[gamma*Uclock-xi*(tpool-1)]*v_i\n       +xi*r_i^pool*(Uclock-(p+s))-tau*rho_i-2alpha_i. (7.10)\n\nThis includes the actual initial clock t_0=1 in BOTH appearances of\n(tpool-1) and (p+s); omitting it would change the optimum row.\nAt query1, divide by the positive u_1 and use v_1/u_1=-rminus:\n\n    nu^B_1/u_1=kappa*(t_1-tbar)\n       +[-gamma*rminus+xi/(U-u_0)]*Uclock\n       +xi*rminus*(tpool-1)-xi*(p+s)/(U-u_0)\n       -tau/U-2delta.                                (7.11)\n\nAll signs in this expression can be bounded uniformly. Since b/a<4,\nalpha_0<p/8, and gamma<2,\n\n    xi=gamma*(bp/a+alpha_0)<2*(4p+p/8)<9p.           (7.12)\n\nAlso U-u_0>7/3-1/8>2. With p<1/128, (7.12) implies\nxi/(U-u_0)<9p/2<1<gamma*rminus. Thus the coefficient of Uclock in\n(7.11) is negative; Uclock>0. The terms -xi*(p+s)/(U-u_0), -tau/U and\n-2delta are nonpositive, with the first and last strictly negative.\nFurther,\n\n    tpool < tbar/(1-rho_0),\n    xi*rminus*tpool < (9p)*(3/2)*(10/9)*tbar\n                                      =15p*tbar.    (7.13)\n\nSince t_1<tbar/2 by (6.9), dropping the negative terms in (7.11) gives\n\n    nu^B_1/u_1<(-kappa/2+15p)*tbar<0.               (7.14)\n\nFor the final strict sign, (7.9) gives kappa/2>7/24, whereas\n15p<15/128<7/24. All denominators and u_1,tbar are positive.\n\nCombining (7.8) and (7.14) covers EVERY kappa>0,tau>=0, with no assumption\non PSD or the first-row signs: either lambda_(z,z-2)<0, or\nlambda_(star,1)=nu^B_1<0. At equality kappa=gamma*U*rplus^2, (7.9) and\n(7.14) still apply. This excludes the WHOLE displayed Law B domain at any\nq, in particular every full-PSD member at qtar, including (7.6).\n\nThis conflict concerns the literal balanced correction (7.1). Another\nbalanced matrix can change the pair (7.8), the optimum row (7.11), or both.\nNo universal exclusion of balanced re-selection is asserted.\n\n## 8. Boundary, sign and scope audit\n\n* All enclosing integers m>=1536 are treated with the SAME beta,c,a at\n  every child. No finite numerical horizon test or reset is used.\n* The probabilities at every original queried target and at the optimum\n  are strictly positive. The actual terminal gradient remains free, with\n  its exact square retained. No earlier remote row is removed.\n* The variance metric controls all n-1 independent detail directions.\n  The independent common direction and its real derivative are retained.\n* Every finite and optimum coefficient has a literal scalar formula.\n  Terminal and outgoing-optimum signs pass for both laws. Incoming-optimum\n  and finite signs fail as proved; no signed row is called a certificate.\n* tau=0 is allowed and covered; kappa>0 is necessary for the specified\n  positive metric. Equality in PSD and in the tail sign has been covered.\n* Balanced B_B e=0 is checked explicitly, and the initial clock contribution\n  is retained in the exact incoming-optimum formula. This is not the old\n  isotropic endpoint correction or the old physical-tree uniform pool.\n* The first-row invariant includes d_0. Failure of these\n  two coefficient laws gives no lower bound on W and no global no-certificate\n  theorem. Fact931's missing full-function hypothesis remains unproved.\n\nThe row identities and inequalities above have received a textual author\naudit only. {"id": "abd79aa1bbe448d0", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-02T21:29:45.668423+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "dead_end", "claim": "max85 scoped closeout: positive endpoint incoming load and independent clock-zero common square do not rescue Law I's first-row clock; Law II repairs that moment with terminal concentration and penultimate metric mass but fails a remote last-row clock. Change a tested terminal/metric/load/common-column moment; full architecture remains OPEN.", "evidence": "# Independent common square, positive incoming load, and two row-clock obstructions\n\nJOINT UNVERIFIED analytical report. Worker max, invocation85.\nOriginal exact-profile minimax problem OPEN. No new fact or formal submission.\n\n## 1. Outcome and exact scope\n\nThis report jointly rederives the enlarged common-square architecture and\nall its original interpolation coefficients. The paid zero-sum column is\nzeta, whereas the actual skew divergence is\n\n    d=zeta+(p-s)(v-z).\n\nSeparating z from v changes the architecture, but does not grant free\nsign-feasible transport. The outgoing optimum vector depends on zeta,\nnot on d alone. Both the incoming total/clock moments and the complete\nfirst-row total/clock moments are derived below for GENERAL z.\n\nLaw I uses a clock-zero endpoint common column independent of v, a\nstrictly positive incoming optimum load nu=(1/64)e_0 at the INITIAL\nquery, and an explicitly scaled positive source/last/tail metric. Its full\npolynomial is PSD below the target and all optimum signs pass. Its\nfirst-row total has the required sign. Nevertheless its endpoint clock\ndefect is negative uniformly in every enclosing m>=1536, for every\nskew completion preserving the selected actions. In the ideal endpoint\nmetric, EVERY compatible endpoint incoming load cancels exactly from\nthis defect. The positive load in this law therefore changes individual\nterms without repairing their net obstruction.\n\nLaw II changes terminal probabilities to the actual last queried index\nand adds a specified metric mass1/16 at the penultimate query. It keeps\nthe independent clock-zero common column and the positive incoming\nload1/64 at index0. This changes the failing moment: the full first-row\nTWO-moment screen now passes, as do full PSD and all optimum signs.\nHowever a different invariant, the last queried row tested against the\nPENULTIMATE clock, is strictly less than -1/16. It forces a negative\noriginal finite multiplier from the last query to an earlier-than-\npenultimate query. Every balanced correction annihilating e and t\npreserves this obstruction.\n\nThese are exclusions of TWO fully specified laws, including every\nremaining balanced skew in each law. They are not an impossibility\nresult for the general architecture or for the full dual cone. No\nformal residual vector or negative coefficient is presented as an\ninterpolable primal trajectory. No full-class upper bound on W, and\ntherefore no resolution of the original problem, has been obtained.\n\nThis is ONE joint proof draft. All new calculations are unverified;\nnone is adopted as a separately accepted premise. The only accepted\nfacts used are1252b8823bb5a290 and931b9653ce0f7201 at the scopes specified\nbelow. The older reports and the main advisory supplied directions only.\n\n## 2. Problem, accepted interfaces, and physical word\n\nFor a predetermined nonnegative word h of length n and smoothness L>0,\nW_n(h) is the supremum of\n2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite Euclidean\ndimension, convex differentiable L-smooth f with an attained minimizer,\nand x_0!=x_star, under x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R.\nThe original question is whether W_n_star/U_n^ZJ tends to1, where\nW_n_star=inf_(h>=0)W_n(h), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, and the original all-split\nbenchmark has\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt(x^2+6xy+y^2))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)).\n\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the signed residual orientation and\nfull terminal-square certificate interpretation. Only incoming edges\nat the ACTUAL unqueried terminal may be deleted; no queried-prefix\ncoordinate or constraint is removed. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 supplies\nthe word below, its mass formula, and B_n/D<47/50. Only CONDITIONAL on\nthe still-missing full-class W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D) for every m>=1536 does\nit imply the799/800 separation. That missing hypothesis is not proved.\n\nFix arbitrary integer m>=1536 and keep its parameters in EVERY child:\n\n    eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,\n    b=1+a, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), j<m,\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, l=n-1, r=l-1,\n    S=sum_(i<n)h_i, D=1+2S, p=1/(1+S), qtar=17/(16D).\n\nThe indices l,r are the last and penultimate QUERIED samples, different\nfrom the actual terminal n. We use the following short local scalar\naliases throughout this report. Their public glossary names carry85:\np=p85, s=s85, qtar=q85, T=T85, U=U85, J=J85, P=P85, H=H85,\nX=X85, r=r85. These explicitly scoped aliases do not change any\nexisting project-glossary definition. The matrix for the chronological\ncoordinate change is Ltri, distinct from scalar T; a superscript T\nmeans transpose. In Section3 only, Len is an integer suffix length.\n\nThe physical last three steps are (b,1+beta,b). Put hgap=1+beta=2-eps.\nElementary bounds from m>=1536 are\n\n    12/5<c<5/2, 7/8<beta<1, 1/3<a<1/2, 4/3<b<3/2.\n\nThe accepted mass formula, also obtained by substitution in\n1+S_(j+1)=2(1+S_j)+a*c^j, is\n\n    1+S_j=(1-vword)2^j+vword*c^j,\n    vword=beta/[c(c-2)] in(0,1).\n\nConsequently\n\n    2^m<1+S<c^m, c^(-m)<p<2^(-m)<2^(-20).             (2.1)\n\nLet e be the all-ones column on the n queries and e_i its coordinate\ncolumns. Define clocks\n\n    t_i=1+sum_(j<i)h_j, T=t_l=1/p-b,\n    Delta=T-1=1/p-b-1, t_r=T-hgap.\n\nThey are strictly increasing, t_0=1 and t_1=1+b. In particular\n\n    3/(4p)<Delta<1/p, Delta-b>1/(2p),\n    0<hgap/Delta<4p<1/128.                            (2.2)\n\n## 3. Tail quantities and the bounds actually needed\n\nWrite sbit(L) for the binary digit sum. For0<=L<=n put\n\n    rtail_L=product_(j=n-L)^(n-1)(h_j-1)\n           =beta^L*c^(-sbit(L)),\n    s=rtail_n=beta^n/c^m,\n    k_i=h_i product_(i<j<n)(1-h_j),\n    u_i=|k_i|, U=sum_i u_i, rho_i=u_i/U.\n\nThe suffix formula follows because h_(n-j)-1=a*c^v2(j), and\nsum_(j=1)^L v2(j)=L-sbit(L). With L=l-i one has\n\n    u_i=rtail_L+rtail_(L+1), k_i=(-1)^i u_i,\n    u_0=(b/a)s, u_l=b, u_r=a(1+beta), u_(l-2)=ba beta,\n    U>7/3.                                          (3.1)\n\nFor the last inequality the last three terms exceed4/3,5/8,7/18,\nwhose sum169/72 exceeds7/3. Every rho_i is strictly positive.\nThe following source bound is especially useful:\n\n    0<s<u_0<p^2/64, rho_0<1/10, s<p.                (3.2)\n\nFor a symbolic proof,2^m-1>16m^2 for m>=16 by induction, starting at16.\nAlso beta^(-4m)>(1+1/(4m))^(4m)>=2, so beta^(4m)<1/2.\nThus beta^n<2^(-4m), while b/a<4 and p>c^(-m) yield\nu_0/p^2<4 beta^n c^m<4(5/32)^m<1/64. The other inequalities follow.\nNo arithmetic, recurrence, or trajectory was evaluated by a program.\n\nTelescoping the terminal update gives\n\n    e^T k=1+s, Ltri^T k=h, t^T k=S,                  (3.3)\n\nwhere Ltri_ii=1, Ltri_ij=h_j for j<i, and zero otherwise.\nFor Law I let tbar=sum rho_i t_i and set\n\n    alpha=(1-p)rho, V=t^T(k-alpha)=(1-p)(1/p-tbar).\n\nThe necessary uniform bounds are\n\n    32<V<512m^3, p V^2<1/1024.                       (3.4)\n\nHere is the full derivation, to avoid importing the previous unverified\nreport. The binary product and its digit-weighted sum give\n\n    Ztail=sum_(L=0)^n rtail_L\n         =product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1+beta^(2^j)/c),\n    U=2Ztail-1-s,\n    Mtail/Ztail=sum_(j=0)^(m-1)\n                    2^j beta^(2^j)/(c+beta^(2^j)),\n    Mtail=sum_(L=0)^n L rtail_L.\n\nChoose j with m/2<2^j<=m. Bernoulli's inequality gives beta^(2^j)>=3/4,\nand c+beta^(2^j)<7/2; hence Mtail/Ztail>3m/28. Also ns<1. Therefore\n\n    sum_i rho_i(l-i)>(Mtail/Ztail-1)/2>3m/56-1/2.\n\nThe suffix after query i has mass at least b+(l-i). As1-p>1/2,\nthe formula for V makes V>32 for m>=1536.\nFor the upper bound, Len=L+1 final steps lie in H_d where d is the least\ninteger with2^d>Len. Thus their mass is smaller than c^d<(2^d)^2<=4Len^2.\nSince u_i<2 beta^L and U>2,\n\n    V<4 sum_(L>=0)(L+1)^2 beta^L\n      =4(1+beta)/(1-beta)^3<512m^3.\n\nThe series identity follows by multiplying by(1-beta)^3.\nFinally2^m>2^30 m^6 for m>=128: the base compares2^128 with2^72,\nand the induction step follows from\n(1+1/m)^6<=sum_(j>=0)(6/m)^j<=64/61<2.\nUsing p<2^(-m) gives pV^2<2^(-12)<1/1024.\n\nLaw II instead chooses alpha=(1-p)e_l. It has\n\n    V=b(1-p), alpha_0=0, v_0=u_0, v_l=a+p,\n    0<V<3/2, pV^2<1/1024.                            (3.5)\n\nIn BOTH laws alpha_star=p, v=k-alpha, E=p+s, F=p-s>0,\neta=F/E, e^T v=E, and\n\n    R=Ltri^T v,\n    R_i=h_i(p+sum_(j<=i)alpha_j)-alpha_i>0.            (3.6)\n\nIndeed R_i=h_i(p+sum_(j<i)alpha_j)+(h_i-1)alpha_i.\nMoreover0<v_0<u_0 in Law I, v_0=u_0 in Law II, and R_0<2p in both.\nFor Law I use alpha_0=((1-p)/U)u_0, with (1-p)/U<3/7.\n\n## 4. FULL original-space identity with an independent common column\n\nNormalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Keep x_0,g_0,...,g_n\narbitrary and independent, substituting ONLY the actual GD recursion.\nThe accepted residual is\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nFor i<n set y_i=x_i-g_i and delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2. Then\n\n    y=e*x_0-Ltri*g,\n    g=Ltri^(-1)(e*x_0-y),\n    x_n=-s*x_0+k^T y.                                (4.1)\n\nLtri is invertible with diagonal1. More explicitly, with\npi_i=product_(j<i)(1-h_j),\n\n    g_i=pi_i*x_0-y_i\n             +sum_(j<i)h_j product_(j<j'<i)(1-h_j')y_j.\n\nThus this change spans ALL original queried-gradient directions.\nThe actual terminal gradient remains independently free.\n\nChoose any z with e^T z=E, positive weights w_i, and zero-sum zeta.\nLet Ww=sum_i w_i and define\n\n    K=diag(w)-ww^T/Ww,\n    P=K+vv^T+eta zz^T,\n    d=zeta+F(v-z),\n    A^T=-A, Ae=d, N=Ltri^T(P+A).                     (4.2)\n\nThe complete signed multiplier array is\n\n    lambda_(n,star)=2p, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(j,n)=0 for j in {star,0,...,l},\n    lambda_(i,j)=-N_ij for distinct i,j<n,\n    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i=(Ne)_i,\n    lambda_(star,i)=nu_i=(N^T e)_i-2alpha_i.           (4.3)\n\nDiagonal multipliers vanish. In scalar entries,\n\n    N_ij=K_ij+A_ij+h_i sum_(r'>i)(K_(r',j)+A_(r',j))\n                          +R_i v_j+eta Zout_i z_j,\n    Zout=Ltri^T z.                                  (4.4)\n\nThe formula defines every original ordered pair; none is hidden behind\nan existence assertion. The finite prefix outgoing total at i is N_ii,\nits incoming total is N_ii-2alpha_i, and the actual terminal adds incoming\n2alpha_i. Hence finite-node divergence is zero, terminal divergence2,\nand optimum divergence-2. All sampled values cancel in\n\n    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij.\n\nFor prefix nodes including star,\nQ_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>; the remaining prefix quadratic is\n-g^T N y. Using(4.1) it is\n-<x_0,(Pe-d)^T y>+y^T P y. The terminal expansion, with -2f_n\nincluded, is\n||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2-||-s*x_0+v^T y||^2-2sum alpha_i delta_i.\nThose delta terms cancel the prefix value terms. Now\n\n    Pe=Ev+Fz,\n    2s v-Pe+d=-2Fz+zeta,\n    eta E^2=p^2-s^2.\n\nSubstitution proves the COMPLETE identity\n\n    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2+y^T K y\n         +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2\n         +(q-p^2)||x_0||^2+<x_0,zeta^T y>.           (4.5)\n\nLet ybar_w=sum w_i y_i/Ww. As e^T zeta=0, completion gives\n\n    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2\n       +sum_i w_i||y_i-ybar_w+(zeta_i/(2w_i))*x_0||^2\n       +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2\n       +(q-p^2-J)||x_0||^2,\n    J=(1/4)sum_i zeta_i^2/w_i.                      (4.6)\n\nThis pays the initial charge ONCE. It retains every detail, the common\nsquare and its derivative, and the actual terminal square.\nFull PSD is equivalent to q>=p^2+J. For necessity take scalar x_0=1,\ny_i=c0-zeta_i/(2w_i), where\nc0=1+[sum_i z_i zeta_i/(2w_i)]/E, and g_n=-s+v^T y.\nEvery displayed square vanishes. Reconstruct the queried gradients by\n(4.1). This is a formal-space test, not a primal interpolation claim.\nIn particular g_n need NOT equal p when z and v differ.\n\nFor the actual common queried-gradient direction g_i=G, y_i=x_0-t_i G.\nPut V=t^T v, Z=t^T z, theta=t^T zeta, X=t^T Kt. The full prefix remainder\nis exactly\n\n    (q-p^2)||x_0||^2-theta<x_0,G>\n                      +(X+eta Z^2)||G||^2.          (4.7)\n\nThe real common derivative theta has not been discarded.\n\n## 5. General optimum moments, complete first-row moments, literal skew\n\nFrom Pe+d=2p v+zeta, the outgoing optimum vector is\n\n    mu=2pR+Ltri^T zeta.                              (5.1)\n\nThe incoming vector is\n\n    nu=Kt+Vv+eta Zz-At-2alpha.                       (5.2)\n\nSince e^T A=-d^T and t^T At=0, its EXACT moments are\n\n    M0=e^T nu=2pV+theta-2(1-p),\n    M1=t^T nu=X+V^2+eta Z^2-2alpha^T t.              (5.3)\n\nA nonnegative nu necessarily has M0>=0 and M0<=M1<=T M0.\nConversely the isolated two-moment problem has the nonnegative endpoint\nsolution\n\n    nu_target=((T M0-M1)/Delta)e_0\n                          +((M1-M0)/Delta)e_l.      (5.4)\n\nThis is not sufficient for the finite multiplier signs.\nFor ANY specified compatible nu_target let\n\n    rvec=Kt+Vv+eta Zz-2alpha-nu_target,\n    tmean=(e^T t)/n, cvec=t-tmean*e, Ve=cvec^T cvec>0,\n    A0=(d e^T-e d^T)/n,\n    fvec=rvec-tmean*d+(d^T t)e/n,\n    A=A0+(fvec cvec^T-cvec fvec^T)/Ve.               (5.5)\n\nThe two moment identities imply e^T rvec=-d^T t and t^T rvec=0.\nThus e^T fvec=0 and cvec^T fvec=0. Multiplication shows Ae=d and\nAt=rvec, with both terms skew. This is a literal entrywise completion.\nEvery other completion is A+C with C^T=-C and Ce=Ct=0.\nSuch C leaves the polynomial, mu,nu and all moment tests below unchanged.\n\nFor the first row define\n\n    S0=sum_(j>0)N_0j,\n    P0=sum_(j>0)(t_j-1)N_0j,\n    G0=P0-Delta*S0=sum_(j>0)(t_j-T)N_0j.\n\nThe first row of Ltri^T is b e^T-a e_0^T. Write\n\n    Lcol=Pe-d=2s v+2F z-zeta.\n\nThen N_0j=b Lcol_j-a(P_0j+A_0j), and N_00=b Lcol_0-aP_00.\nAs mu_0=2pR_0-a zeta_0, the complete general first-row sum is\n\n    S0=aP_00+zeta_0+2pR_0-2bs v_0-2bF z_0.          (5.6)\n\nUsing(P+A)t=2Pt-2alpha-nu yields the complete clock identity\n\n    P0=b(2sV+2FZ-theta)-2a(Pt)_0\n                                  +2a alpha_0+a nu_0-mu_0,\n    (Pt)_0=(Kt)_0+Vv_0+eta Zz_0.                    (5.7)\n\nThese formulas explicitly include BOTH optimum directions.\nIf all finite lambda_(0,j)>=0, equivalently N_0j<=0, then necessarily\n\n    S0<=0, Delta*S0<=P0<=b*S0.                      (5.8)\n\nIndeed b<=t_j-1<=Delta for all j>0. When S0=0 all off-diagonals must\nvanish. These are exact invariant screens, not sufficient row signs.\n\nThere is another invariant at the actual last query l. Its row in\nLtri^T is e_l^T, so N_ll=P_ll, (Nt)_l=2(Pt)_l-2alpha_l-nu_l.\nTesting its earlier entries against t_r, the penultimate clock, gives\n\n    Hlast=sum_(j<l)(t_j-t_r)N_lj\n         =2(Pt)_l-2alpha_l-nu_l-t_r mu_l-hgap*P_ll.  (5.9)\n\nNonpositive N_lj require Hlast>=0, since t_j<=t_r for j<l.\nA negative Hlast forces N_lj>0 at some j<r, not merely at r.\n\n## 6. The shared explicit common column, incoming load, and paid imbalance\n\nBoth laws fix the genuinely independent common column\n\n    z=E(T e_0-e_l)/Delta, e^T z=E, t^T z=0.          (6.1)\n\nIt differs from v: for example every odd interior v_i is negative and\nnonzero, whereas z_i=0 there. The independence is not a scalar rescaling.\nSet the NONZERO incoming load\n\n    ellload=1/64, nu_target=ellload*e_0.              (6.2)\n\nFor each law's own alpha,V define\n\n    theta0=2(1-p)-2pV=2p alpha^T t,\n    theta=theta0+ellload,\n    Xreq=theta0/p-V^2+ellload.                       (6.3)\n\nSelect positive base weights what_i as specified for that law and put\n\n    tbase=sum what_i t_i/sum what,\n    Vbase=sum what_i(t_i-tbase)^2,\n    scale=Xreq/Vbase, w_i=scale*what_i,\n    K=diag(w)-ww^T/sum w,\n    zeta=(theta/Xreq)Kt.                             (6.4)\n\nThis fixes every scale from the actual word before row-sign work.\nIt gives t^T Kt=Xreq, e^T zeta=0 and t^T zeta=theta. Moreover\n\n    J=theta^2/(4Xreq).                              (6.5)\n\nIndeed(Kt)_i=w_i(t_i-tbar_w), whose squared inverse-weight sum is\nXreq. Weighted Cauchy--Schwarz shows the same law minimizes this charge\nat fixed theta for its metric. By(5.3), M0=M1=ellload. Thus(6.2) is\nexactly compatible. Define d by(4.2), A by(5.5), then EVERY scalar\nmultiplier by(4.3)--(4.4). The literal law sets C=0; our obstructions\nwill cover every extra skew C annihilating e and t.\n\n### 6.1 Positive metric, full PSD, all optimum signs\n\nFor both laws1<theta0<2. In Law I use V>32 and pV^2<1/1024 to get\npV<1/32768 and then theta0>1. In Law II use V<3/2 and(2.1).\nConsequently\n\n    Xreq>(1023/1024)*theta0/p>0,\n    Xreq<2/p+ellload, 1<theta<3, theta/Xreq>p.        (6.6)\n\nThe last follows because theta-pXreq=ellload(1-p)+pV^2>0.\nEvery base weight is positive because p^2 rho_i>0, and the two unit\nendpoint masses make Vbase>0. Hence all metric weights are positive.\nThe full charge satisfies\n\n    J < [theta0*p/4]*(1+ellload/theta0)^2*(1024/1023)\n      < (4225/8184)p,\n    p^2+J <(17/32)p<qtar.                            (6.7)\n\nFor the second line use p<1/128 and\n4225/8184<67/128, equivalent to540800<548328.\nThus full PSD holds strictly below the requested target for BOTH laws.\nNo interpolation signs are inferred from PSD alone.\n\nThe terminal multipliers are nonnegative, and nu is exactly ellload at\nindex0 and zero elsewhere. All outgoing mu_i are STRICTLY positive.\nTo prove this, zeta_i has the sign of t_i-tbar_w, crossing only once\nfrom negative to positive, and has zero sum. Every proper suffix sum\nis positive: if i is before the crossing, use the negative preceding\nprefix and zero total; if i is after it, every remaining term is\npositive. If zeta_i<0 then sum_(j>i)zeta_j>=-zeta_i, whence\n\n    (Ltri^T zeta)_i=zeta_i+h_i sum_(j>i)zeta_j\n                                     >=(h_i-1)(-zeta_i)>0.\n\nIf zeta_i>=0 the same expression is positive, including i=l because\nzeta_l>0. Equation(5.1) proves all mu_i>0.\n\n## 7. Exact endpoint-clock reduction and incoming-load cancellation\n\nFor (6.1) put Y=-(Kt)_0=w_0(tbar_w-1)>0. Then zeta_0=-theta Y/Xreq.\nWrite\n\n    K_00=Xreq/Delta^2+eK, Y=Xreq/Delta+eY.\n\nSubstituting(5.6)--(5.7), z_0=ET/Delta and Z=0 gives\n\n    G0=Gendpoint+Epert,\n    Epert=a(2eY-Delta eK)+theta(Delta-a)eY/Xreq,\n    Gendpoint=(a/Delta)[Xreq-T theta-(p^2-s^2)T^2]\n       +a nu_0+2bs(V-ET)\n       +v_0[2apT+2bsDelta-2aV]-aDelta v_0^2+2a alpha_0. (7.1)\n\nThis follows by expanding the metric part as\n a(2Y-Delta K_00)+(a-Delta)zeta_0-btheta,\nand using R_0=bE-a v_0 in the remaining terms.\nFor the endpoint reduction with zeta=(theta/Xreq)Kt and ANY\ncompatible endpoint load, not just(6.2), write its total M0\nand moment M1, so theta=theta0+M0 and\nXreq=theta0/p-V^2+M1. Endpoint support means\nM1-T M0=-Delta nu_0. Consequently\n\n    Xreq-T theta=btheta0-V^2-Delta nu_0,\n    (a/Delta)(Xreq-Ttheta)+a nu_0\n                                  =(a/Delta)(btheta0-V^2). (7.2)\n\nThus EVERY endpoint incoming load cancels out of Gendpoint exactly.\nFor our fixed nonzero load, therefore,\n\n    Gendpoint=(a/Delta)[btheta0-V^2-(p^2-s^2)T^2]\n       +2bs(V-ET)\n       +v_0[2apT+2bsDelta-2aV]-aDelta v_0^2+2a alpha_0. (7.3)\n\nA positive load is not intrinsically useless: it changes other rows,\nthe charge, and the metric perturbation. Equation(7.2) states only this\nprecise endpoint cancellation and does not exclude distributed metrics.\n\n## 8. Law I: tail terminal probabilities, source/last metric, positive load\n\nFix alpha=(1-p)rho and the complete choices(6.1)--(6.4) with\n\n    omega=p^2, what=e_0+e_l+omega*rho.               (8.1)\n\nEquations(4.3)--(4.4),(5.5) specify ALL multipliers. Full PSD and every\noptimum sign already follow jointly from Section6.\nFor exact metric estimates put xclock_i=(t_i-1)/Delta in[0,1],\nrbar=sum rho_i xclock_i, and define\n\n    gbase=Vbase/Delta^2,\n    fbase=(1+omega rho_0)(1+omega rbar)/(2+omega).\n\nRetaining the two unit endpoints in the variance minimum gives\ngbase>=1/2; centering at1/2 gives gbase<=1/2+omega/4.\nMoreover1/2-omega/4<=fbase<=1/2+omega and\n\n    K_00/scale=\n       [1+omega+omega^2 rho_0(1-rho_0)]/(2+omega)\n                        in[1/2,1/2+omega/2].\n\nSince Y=(Xreq/Delta)fbase/gbase, these imply\n\n    |eY|<=4Xreq*omega/Delta,\n    |eK|<=2Xreq*omega/Delta^2. In Law II the same\nbound K_00/Y<9/(8Delta) follows from the displayed base weights: the noninitial\nbase mass is at most1+1/16+omega, while its normalized clock sum is\nat least1. Hence, in either case,\n\n    aK_00/Y<3p/4, theta/Xreq>p,\n    Y>1/3,\n    S0<-p/12+7p^2<-p/16<0. Then\nY=(Xreq/Delta)f/H>1/3 by(6.6),(2.2).\n\nNevertheless G0 is strictly negative in Law I. In(7.3), discard all\nnonpositive terms, use a>1/3, Delta<1/p, theta0<2, Delta>1/(2p),\nand the source bounds to obtain\n\n    Gendpoint<3p-(p/3)V^2+2bsV\n                    +v_0(2apT+2bsDelta)+2a alpha_0\n              <4p-(p/3)V^2.                         (8.5)\n\nIndeed2bsV<3u_0 V<3p/64 because pV<1;\n2apT<1, 2bsDelta<3s/p<1, and2a alpha_0<u_0.\nThus the remaining source contributions are smaller than p.\nBy(8.3), p<2^(-20) and V>32,\n\n    G0 < p[4-V^2/3+40p] < p[5-V^2/3] <0.           (8.6)\n\nThis contradicts the required G0>=0 in(5.8). For EVERY extra skew\nC with Ce=Ct=0, some original finite lambda_(0,j),1<=j<l, is negative.\nThe j=l term has zero coefficient in G0 and cannot create this failure.\nThe nonzero incoming load did not repair the whole-law obstruction.\n\n## 9. Law II: terminal probability at last query and penultimate metric mass\n\nThe second and FINAL concrete law changes BOTH the terminal clock and\nthe interior metric moment:\n\n    alpha=(1-p)e_l, omega=p^2, sigma=1/16,\n    what=e_0+e_l+sigma e_r+omega rho. Remaining interface and bounded handoff\n\nThe independent common column is a valid enlargement at the algebraic\nlevel; the paid charge is J(zeta), not J(d). The outgoing optimum vector,\nincoming total/clock moments, first-row total/clock, and last-row\npenultimate-clock tests are now all explicit. A future law must satisfy\nALL of them along with every individual finite and optimum sign.\n\nFor Law I, adding an endpoint incoming load without changing the\nrelevant metric moment cannot repair its first-row obstruction. For\nLaw II, the terminal concentration and late interior weight repair that\nfirst-row test but force the remote last-row sign failure. Altering only\na balanced skew Ce=Ct=0 repairs neither law. The remaining freedoms\ninclude changing terminal probabilities, the distribution of metric\nweight, the paid column away from the fixed minimum-energy clock law,\nthe distribution/moments of incoming load, or the independent common\ncolumn away from this clock-zero endpoint choice. {"id": "9e40575809af8a7a", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-02T22:54:45.628252+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "dead_end", "claim": "max87 scoped failure synthesis: prescribed late common and explicit chronological PSD-detail laws cannot keep nu0=1/64 at the target charge.
